# Magic engine bug ledger

| `necroplasm-upkeep-counter-sweep-missing` | RAV/core trigger-effect gap — Necroplasm had only its Dredge/base-creature compatibility slice. Its two simultaneous beginning-of-upkeep abilities require controller-visible trigger ordering and a reusable creature-only sweep that samples the live source's `+1/+1` counter quantity when it resolves. | Fixed as bounded compatibility; red `f69b5e9b`, green `3c35477d` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test necroplasm_upkeep_red -- --nocapture`. It failed 0/1 at `Necroplasm must expose its first beginning-of-upkeep ability`. The green repair adds the target-free counter-sweep effect, two normal Upkeep bindings, and a public `order_triggers` scenario action. Focused trace: `DecisionOpened(TriggeredAbilityOrder) → TriggeredAbilityOrderChosen(sweep,counter) → TriggeredAbilityStacked ×2 → CounterPlaced(+1/+1,1) → CardDestroyed(mana value 1) → AbilityResolved`; it remains outside the positive manifest because the source-departure last-known-counter case is deliberately not claimed. |

| `rav-necroplasm-scenario-count-drift` | RAV shown-fixture reconciliation gap — the new policy-submitted Necroplasm simultaneous-trigger trace executes, but the shown-index count still names the preceding corpus size. | Fixed; red `1b8db4c1`, green `56183c50` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test necroplasm_scenario_count_red -- --nocapture`. It failed 0/1: `left: 176`, `right: 177` at the shown scenario-count assertion. The checked public corpus and deterministic library contract now both declare 177 scenarios. |

| `necroplasm-source-departure-last-known-counter-value` | RAV/core fidelity boundary — the source-present counter sweep intentionally no-ops when Necroplasm leaves before its trigger resolves; full rules fidelity needs an exact last-known `+1/+1` counter snapshot for that departed source incarnation. | Open | The bounded green implementation `3c35477d` guards the sweep with the source's live battlefield incarnation, so it cannot accidentally read a later re-entry's counters. This preserves state-machine safety but does not implement the printed source-departure interaction; Necroplasm remains partial and outside `RAV_FULL_FIDELITY_DEFINITION_IDS`. |

| `consult-the-necrosages-cast-modal-choice-missing` | RAV/core catalog-execution gap — Consult the Necrosages was catalog-only because a spell cast could not retain the caster's selected one-of-two targeted modes through payment, stack placement, resolution, and event auditing. | Fixed as bounded compatibility; red `d5fdcc02`, green `046a5493` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test consult_necrosages_red -- --nocapture` failed 0/1 at `Consult the Necrosages definition exists`. The green path adds policy action `CastWithMode`, an invariant-audited stack `chosen_modal_mode`, `SpellModeChosen`, and one target-player draw-two or discard-two effect bundle. It intentionally does not promote the card to the full manifest. |

| `consult-the-necrosages-recipient-private-discard-choice-missing` | RAV/core fidelity gap — Consult's target-player discard-two branch used the deterministic oldest-hand-card fallback instead of allowing the target player to privately choose two cards. | Fixed; red `c51aa916`, green `9486faf8`, hardening `354820ab` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test consult_private_discard_choice_red -- --nocapture`. It failed 0/1 because no recipient-private decision opened; the printed trace recorded `CardDiscarded(ObjectId(3))` then `CardDiscarded(ObjectId(4))`, the oldest target-hand cards, before `SpellResolved`. The green continuation captures the stack-item id, source incarnation/controller/ability, recipient, fixed count, and exact hand object/incarnation snapshot. Its green trace is `DecisionOpened(Private, recipient, 2) → DecisionCompleted → CardDiscarded ×2 → CardMoved(Graveyard) ×2 → SpellResolved`; selected candidate identities never enter decision receipts. Wrong-player and stale-id submissions are regression-tested atomic no-ops. |

| `public-game-fields-can-bypass-transition-machine` | Engine API-encapsulation weakness — a caller can directly rewrite a public `PlayerState::life` field in a live game without a rule action or canonical receipt, yielding a shape-valid state that `validate_invariants` cannot distinguish from a legal transition. | Open; red regression pending | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test state_provenance_boundary_red -- --nocapture`. It fails 0/1 with `fabricated life transition: audit=Ok(()); life=13; events_unchanged=true`; the only receipts remain `StepBegan` for turn-one Untap and Upkeep. The test intentionally requires the audit to reject the receipt-free live life change. This is a provenance—not shape—gap: the public fixture API exposes `Game::players` and every `PlayerState` field, while `event_log_integrity` can only detect edits to the log itself. |

| `combat-mutual-lethal-block-history-provenance` | Core combat state-machine defect — after a legal blocker assignment, mutually lethal combat damage changed both combatants' zone identities; the stale assignment then failed the block-history audit and atomically rolled back the final priority pass. | Fixed; red `d61af4e2`, green `2d70ad47` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test blocker_history_mutual_lethal_red -- --nocapture`; it failed 0/1 with `left: Err("current blocker assignment lacks block-history provenance")`, `right: Ok(())`, after `BlockersDeclared { assignments: [(ObjectId(17), ObjectId(18))] }` and the first post-block `PriorityPassed`. The broader deterministic policy fuzz independently failed at seed `0xA11CE5E012345678`, operation 93. The narrow green repair removes ordinary battlefield departures from live combat membership before advancing their zone incarnation, retaining immutable exact-incarnation history only for delayed effects; the focused two-order fixture passes 2/2, with both `StateBasedAction { reason: "creature has lethal damage" }` receipts and both graveyard moves retained. The fuzz seed and full engine test package pass. |

| `bottled-cloister-upkeep-linked-hand-exile-missing` | RAV artifact/core coverage gap — Bottled Cloister was catalog-only because upkeep triggers observed only active-player permanents, and the engine had no exact-source private hand-exile group that could return captured cards before drawing. | Fixed; red `cc8e018e`, green `ce00b925` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test bottled_cloister_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` failed 0/1 at `Bottled Cloister definition exists`. The reusable repair adds `BeginningOfOpponentsUpkeep`, exact source/incarnation hand-exile groups, lifecycle events, state-machine invariant audits, and source-departure cleanup. Focused trace: `TriggeredAbilityStacked(opponent upkeep) → CardMoved(Exile) ×N → HandExiledWithSource → AbilityResolved`; the controller-upkeep trace is `CardMoved(Hand) ×N → LinkedHandExileReturned → CardMoved(Hand draw) → AbilityResolved`. An intervening Smash source departure emits `LinkedHandExileExpired` while the cards stay in exile; every branch ends `validate_invariants()`-valid. |

The fixture-only repair adds fixed ids and first advances the private allocator through an ordinary warmup cast, so the audited states are rejected for their intended reasons rather than identity drift. Focused traces verify the precise X-cost, departed-controller, sorcery-timing, self-target order, unsupported-card, and malformed-resolution errors; `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --tests --no-run` and strict `cargo clippy -p cardbench-magic-engine --all-targets -- -D warnings` pass. No production code changed. |

| `induce-paranoia-countered-controller-mill-missing` | RAV Blue/core stack-resolution gap — Induce Paranoia has no executable definition or spent-blue conditional mill of a countered spell's controller by its mana value | Fixed; red `e6fc2cd1`, green `50f2a715` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test induce_paranoia_red -- --nocapture`. The repair adds the expansion-neutral `CounterTargetPhysicalSpellThenMillItsControllerByManaValueIfManaColorSpent` effect and a `PhysicalSpell` target boundary: it snapshots the lower non-copy spell's controller and mana value before `SpellCountered → CardMoved(Graveyard)`, then mills only if the resolving spell's retained explicit payment includes Blue. Focused engine tests prove Blue and non-Blue branches separately; the RAV trace is `SpellManaPaid([Blue, Blue, Blue]) → SpellCountered(target) → CardMoved(target, Graveyard) → CardMoved(library top, Graveyard) ×2 → SpellResolved(source) → CardMoved(source, Graveyard)`. The targeted invariant audit passes. The green contract also reconciles the previously stale public full-fidelity expected array by restoring its already-promoted Boros Fury-Shield and Light of Sanction identities before adding Induce Paranoia. |

The focused trace is `SpellCast → PriorityPassed ×2 → DecisionOpened(Private) → DecisionCompleted → CardMoved(Hand) → PrivateLibraryTopPartitionResolved(inspected=3) → SpellResolved → CardMoved(source Graveyard)`. | `untap-target-permanent-effect-missing` | Engine stack-effect gap — high severity for any card that must untap an arbitrary permanent rather than only a land or its own source | Fixed; red `cb27e48f`, green `c5feff9f` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test tidewater_minion_red -- --nocapture`. | `departing-player-virtual-spell-copy-remains-on-stack` | Engine multiplayer/stack lifecycle defect — high severity, any virtual spell copy controlled by a player who loses in a continuing multiplayer game | Fixed; red `9c437154`, green `6dc81750` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test virtual_spell_copy_departure_red -- --nocapture`. The copy effect created virtual `ObjectId(3)` above player 0's physical instant. After player 0 reached zero life in a three-player game, SBA logged `PlayerLost` and `ObjectLeftGame` for physical objects but left `StackObject { card: ObjectId(3), controller: PlayerId(0), ... }` live. The regression failed `a player who left the game cannot retain a virtual spell copy on the stack` (0 passed, 1 failed). The repair removes every virtual copy controlled by the departing seat before physical-object cleanup, records `SpellCopyLeftGame { copy, original, controller }`, blocks event-log resets while a copy needs its `SpellCopied` provenance, and audits each copy as live or exactly once terminal. Green trace: `PlayerLost → SpellCopyLeftGame → ObjectLeftGame ×2 → next survivor's Upkeep`; the stack is empty and `validate_invariants()` passes. |
| `generalized-activation-selected-mana-composition-missing` | Engine activation-cost/state-machine gap — high severity, every represented generalized activated cost that also needs controller-selected generic or hybrid mana | Fixed; red `15c99530`, green `9669e6d3` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test generalized_activation_payment_selection_red -- --nocapture`. It failed to compile with `error[E0560]: struct GeneralizedAbilityActivation has no field named mana_payment_selection`, so the public generalized activation request could not carry the explicit generic/hybrid allocation already supported by ordinary bound activation. The green API adds one optional `ManaPaymentSelection` to the existing generalized activation request and delegates to the ordinary selected-spend payment path inside the same atomic transaction. It preserves selected colors as source-incarnation-bound calculated-cost provenance and on the live ability stack object; the invariant audit rejects malformed hybrid symbols, stale cost/activation identities, and a live stack allocation that disagrees with the recorded selection. Focused event evidence is `ActivatedAbilityCostCalculated(payment_selection=[hybrid Blue, generic Red]) → AbilityManaPaid({1}{U/R}) → AbilityLifePaid(2) → AbilityActivated → PriorityPassed ×2 → CardMoved(Hand) → AbilityResolved`; Blue spent on the hybrid symbol makes the conditional draw resolve. An unavailable second Blue selection rolls back mana, life, stack, and receipts atomically. |

| `event-log-reset-strands-live-decision` | Engine state-machine/provenance defect — high severity, every typed no-priority continuation exposed to scenario measurement | Fixed; red `2f027d43`, green `ecf87b8a` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test event_log_reset_pending_decision_red -- --nocapture`. A legal top-library reorder decision was opened, then the public `Game::clear_event_log()` helper erased `DecisionOpened` while leaving the pending continuation live. The invariant audit failed with `Err(IllegalAction("pending decision violates its identity, cardinality, or no-priority boundary"))` and an empty event log. The repair makes reset a receipt-quiescent boundary: it defers without mutating either log whenever clearing would orphan typed decision, activated/triggered stack-ability, permission-timed spell, delayed linked-exile, attached Equipment, or terminal provenance. Focused green trace retains `DecisionOpened(LibraryReorder)`, accepts the original exact decision id, and completes the spell under `validate_invariants()`. |
| `policy-spell-cost-selection-missing` | Engine policy-action / spell-cost gap — high severity, every represented chosen-X or spent-mana-conditional spell | Fixed; red `57e26408`, green `f86d5595` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test policy_cast_payment_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. It failed to compile because `PolicyAction::CastWithPayment` did not exist: a submitted policy had only legacy `Cast`, which routes to `Game::cast_spell` and cannot supply a chosen `{X}` or explicit generic/hybrid allocation. The green action carries one optional X plus one exact allocation and delegates to the existing atomic selected-spend/chosen-X paths, preserving `SpellManaPaid → SpellCast`, stack provenance, and `PolicyMoveKind::Cast`. Focused live-game evidence uses typed basic-land payment sources: the chosen-X trace spends three Black symbols, destroys the mana-value-two target, and rejects an incomplete allocation with no source tap, pool, zone, stack, event-log, or accepted-policy-move mutation; the spent-mana trace pays Black plus Blue and draws exactly once. Audit boundary: RAV has no Phyrexian-mana cost in this set; its represented hybrid, Convoke, creature-sacrifice additional cost, X, and spent-mana conditional forms now have typed cast surfaces. Alternative/rather-than, player-counter, hidden-zone, and generalized nonmana spell costs remain separate unsupported cost families. |
| `damage-replacement-generic-decision-unification-missing` | Engine replacement/state-machine defect — high severity, affected-player choice across prevention/redirection and quantity replacement families | Fixed; red `5cdda9e6`, green `4069a793` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test damage_replacement_generic_decision_red -- --nocapture`. It failed at `damage replacements use the generic decision surface`: competing prevention and redirection opened only the legacy projection, while token/counter replacements used `PendingDecision`. The repair adds a typed `DecisionContinuation::DamageReplacement` with captured spell-source and target incarnations, `ReplacementChoice::Damage` options, and the same stale-safe `DecisionId` submission path. The legacy `ChooseDamageReplacement` action now validates its old fields then delegates to the current generic id. Focused event evidence is `DecisionOpened(Replacement) → DamageReplacementApplied(Redirect) → DamageRedirected → DamageDealtToPlayer → DecisionCompleted(Replacement) → SpellResolved`; prevention has the analogous `DamagePrevented` batch. Boundary: the unified decision representation remains limited to one targeted direct-damage spell, full-event redirects, and the existing shield/protection kinds; partial redirects, optional replacements, and a fully generalized replacement-event algebra remain open. |

| `replacement-chain-affected-player-ordering-missing` | Engine replacement/state-machine defect — high severity, concurrent token/counter multipliers and future prevention/redirection chains | Fixed; red `779f9189`, green `78a1ac5a` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test replacement_chain_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; it initially failed to compile because `ReplacementChoice`, `DecisionKind::Replacement`, `DecisionSelection::Replacements`, and `PendingDecisionView.replacement_candidates` did not exist. The green substrate reuses the id-bearing `PendingDecision` boundary for one-effect token creation and counter placement. It captures source incarnations in every quantity choice and receipt, applies one affected-player-selected option, recomputes live candidates, opens a fresh decision only while two or more remain, automatically applies one remaining candidate, and commits the ordinary token/counter event only after the finite chain ends. Focused trace: `DecisionOpened(1) → ReplacementEffectApplied(1→3) → DecisionCompleted(1) → DecisionOpened(2) → ReplacementEffectApplied(3→6) → DecisionCompleted(2) → ReplacementEffectApplied(6→24) → TokenCreated ×24 → SpellResolved`; the counter path shares that chain and reaches `CounterPlaced(6)`. Existing two-Doubling-Season coverage now submits the public order explicitly. The bounded direct-damage path now shares the same typed decision surface through `ReplacementChoice::Damage`; partial replacement, optional replacements, non-quantity token/counter transformations, and a fully generalized replacement-event algebra remain open. |

| `combat-damage-order-and-deathtouch-missing` | Engine combat/state-machine defect — high severity, every multi-block attacker and every deathtouch source | Fixed; red `3ebb4ef5`, green `8c3b8a70` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test combat_damage_order_deathtouch_red -- --nocapture`. It failed to compile because neither `Keyword::Deathtouch` nor `DecisionKind::CombatDamageOrder` existed. The repair migrates CR 509.2 multi-block ordering to the public generic `PendingDecision` slot: after `BlockersDeclared`, the attacking player must submit one complete blocker permutation per multi-block group before priority; `DecisionOpened → DecisionCompleted → CombatDamageOrderChosen` preserves replay-visible order. Stale, partial, duplicate, foreign, and out-of-group answers remain atomic through the existing id/cardinality boundary. It also records committed deathtouch-damage provenance on the target incarnation, assigns one positive lethal point per blocker, applies the lethal SBA independently of toughness, and clears that provenance at regeneration, cleanup, and battlefield re-entry. Focused trace proves a 7/7 deathtouch trampler ordered against 4/4 then 2/2 blockers deals `1 + 1` and `5` to the defender, after which both blockers reach their ordinary graveyard SBA transition. Boundary: policies still cannot choose arbitrary legal damage amounts, combat prevention/replacement ordering is not yet generalized, and attack/block constraints remain bounded. |

| `control-change-derived-controller-substrate-missing` | Engine continuous-effect / object-control gap — high severity, blocks control-changing cards and corrupts controller-relative rules when approximated | Fixed; red `c2c74d8d`, green `86f0acd4` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test control_change_red -- --nocapture`, which failed to compile because `Effect::GainControlTargetUntilEndOfTurn`, `ContinuousChange::ChangeController`, `Game::controller_of`, and `GameEvent::ControllerChanged` did not exist. The green repair adds owner-indexed battlefield vectors, layer-two timestamped derived control, live `controller_of` projection, public controller-change receipts, cleanup/source-departure reversion, controller-relative target/Aura/SBA/view/untap/combat/trigger/cost/replacement integration, and controller-change summoning-sickness provenance. The focused synthetic contract is 6/6: a stolen permanent appears only in its new controller's view; a temporary spell records `ContinuousEffectCreated → ControllerChanged` and reverses at cleanup; a persistent source departure reverses; a controlled permanent returns to its owner's hand; an Aura whose `ControlledCreature` target ceases to be controlled is SBA-cleaned; and a creature stolen this turn cannot attack. Boundary: only the target-permanent `GainControlTargetUntilEndOfTurn` stack effect is represented so far. Permanent control, exchange, controller-chooses, control-changing Auras, and full layer-2 dependency/copy interactions remain separate core work. |

This is a public discovery ledger for the Rust RAV engine slice. It deliberately
separates engine defects from policy/harness outcomes. Each open engine item is
reproduced by `cargo run -p cardbench-magic-policies --bin rav-engine-audit`
from `varieties/magic`; the audit exits nonzero while an item remains open.

| `trigger-event-recipient-and-opponent-graveyard-provenance-missing` | Engine trigger-scheduler gap — high severity, combat "that creature" effects and card-to-opponent-graveyard triggers | Fixed; red `6efe9093`, green `f6643f10` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test trigger_breadth_red -- --nocapture`. Both synthetic cases failed: combat damage opened an arbitrary `DistinctCreature` target choice rather than retaining its recipient, and a hand-to-opponent-graveyard discard left the observer at `Some(1)` rather than `Some(2)`. The green substrate adds `DealsCombatDamageToCreature` with an exact-recipient/incarnation payload and non-targeting materialized destruction, plus `OpponentCardPutIntoGraveyard` driven by every ordinary graveyard zone transition. Focused trace: `DamageDealtToPermanent(source, recipient) → TriggeredAbilityStacked → CardDestroyed(recipient)`, with no target decision; and `CardDiscarded → CardMoved(Graveyard) → ObjectIncarnationAdvanced → TriggeredAbilityStacked → CounterPlaced`. Binding and live-stack invariants reject escaped runtime provenance effects and ensure a captured recipient cannot affect a later incarnation. Boundary: this does not yet add complete APNAP policy ordering, intervening-if predicates, or the broader full trigger scheduler. |

| `composable-activated-life-counter-return-x-costs-missing` | Engine activation-cost/state-machine gap — high severity, abilities with nonmana payments beyond existing discard/sacrifice/tap fields | Fixed; red `035941c8`, green `783ae78a` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test generalized_activated_costs_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. Before the repair it failed to compile because there was no typed generalized cost profile/binding, policy request, activation API, or life/counter/return/X receipts. The green substrate binds immutable profiles to existing ability ids before a game begins, accepts exact policy-selected counter and permanent objects as one priority action, preflights full mana including `{X}`, life, counter totals, and return selections, then commits all receipts before `AbilityActivated`. Focused event evidence: `AbilityManaPaid({4}) → AbilityLifePaid(2) → CounterRemovedAsAbilityCost(charge,2) → CounterRemoved(charge,2) → ReturnedAsAbilityCost → CardMoved(Hand) → ObjectIncarnationAdvanced → AbilityXCostChosen(3) → AbilityActivated`. The companion adversarial contract selects unaffordable `{X}=4` and proves mana, life, counters, zones, stack, and event log roll back atomically. Boundary: this profile API does not yet compose explicit generic/hybrid mana-color selection with generalized costs, nor does it model alternative/rather-than/conditional-resolution costs, player counters, or costs that choose hidden-zone objects. |

| `copy-layer-copiable-values-missing` | Engine object-characteristics / lifecycle gap — high severity, all permanent and token copy effects | Fixed; red `396f8cd3`, green `01168568` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test copy_semantics_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` failed to compile because `Game::copy_permanent` and the required `PermanentCopied`/`PermanentCopyExpired` receipts did not exist. The green substrate adds an object-local layer-one `CopiableValues` snapshot distinct from derived `Characteristics`: it copies a card definition or token specification, never counters, marked damage, or later-layer effects. It is timestamped and target-incarnation-bound, survives source departure, and expires on target departure. Effective definition lookup safely grants copied definition-bound abilities/static bindings; `AbilityActivated` retains its effective definition so the historical event remains valid after the target resumes its printed identity. The focused trace is `PermanentCopied(source=1,target=2) → AbilityActivated(definition=TST-COPY-SOURCE) → DamageDealtToPlayer → CardDestroyed(source) → CardDestroyed(target) → PermanentCopyExpired(target)`. It also proves a card can copy a Spirit token's values without becoming a token. Boundary: copy effects are currently installed through the explicit public `Game::copy_permanent` transition; spell-copy/casting-permission effects, replacement-of-copy choices, and full copy-effect duration/exception rules remain separate substrates. |
| `unified-decision-identity-and-continuation-missing` | Engine policy-decision state-machine defect — critical severity, every pending public or hidden selection | Fixed; red `b5ffdc1c`, green `0ed11f53`/`a67ef064` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test unified_decision_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` printed `stale unified-decision probe: result=Ok(())` and then `SacrificedByEffect { source: ObjectId(1), player: PlayerId(0), permanent: ObjectId(3) }`: a source/ability/selection action from an earlier upkeep was indistinguishable from a later identical prompt. The repair provides one cloned typed `PendingDecision` slot with a monotonic nonreusable `DecisionId`, typed visibility/options/cardinality/continuation, `PolicyAction::SubmitDecision`, and public `DecisionOpened`/`DecisionCompleted` receipts with no hidden candidates. A stale id now reports `Err(IllegalAction("stale or unknown decision id"))` without changing the view or receipt log; wrong-player, illegal-option, duplicate, min/max, and priority submissions likewise roll back atomically. The first real migrations are public triggered sacrifice plus private triggered discard and library-search continuations. Chord proves an opponent sees neither the generic pending decision nor library candidates, while final `LibrarySearchResolved`, `CardDiscarded`, and `SacrificedByEffect` receipts remain specialized. Focused unified-decision, Chord, Sadistic Augermage, and Vindictive Mob contracts plus strict engine-library Clippy pass. Boundary: targets, optional costs, colors, trigger/replacement ordering, and combat assignment have not yet moved to the generic slot; legacy specialized actions remain compatibility shims. |

| `linked-exile-delayed-return-substrate-missing` | Engine object-identity / delayed-action gap — critical for Aura blink effects and any later linked exile | Fixed; red `e61c160e`, green `d935d778` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test linked_exile_delayed_return_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` failed `left: Some(Graveyard)`, `right: Some(Exile)`: the existing creature-exile instruction orphaned the attached Aura. The repair adds typed, clonable `LinkedExileGroup`/`DelayedAction` state, exact exile-incarnation members, deterministic creature-before-Aura return, and schedule/consume receipts. The green trace records creature `ObjectId(1)` exile incarnation 2 and Aura `ObjectId(2)` exile incarnation 4, then `DelayedActionScheduled`, a lower stale-target spell `SpellCounteredByRules`, and at end step creature incarnation 3 before Aura incarnation 5 / `AuraAttached` / `DelayedActionConsumed`. A member that no longer remains in that exact exile incarnation is skipped; no stable ID alone can revive or reattach it. Focused green regression and strict engine-library Clippy pass. Boundary: this substrate does not yet promote Flickerform or model policy ordering for simultaneous delayed actions. |

## Discovery batch 2026-07-30

| ID | Classification | Status | Reproducible observation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `damage-cannot-be-prevented-incorrectly-bypasses-nonprevention-replacements` | Engine prospective-event/replacement defect — high severity, every unpreventable damage source interacting with redirection | Fixed; red `c2459d19`, green `c6476e92` | The engine's former narrow replacement path treated `Keyword::DamageCannotBePrevented` as an override of both prevention and redirection. That is too broad: the rule excludes prevention only, while a Razia-style damage-redirection replacement remains applicable. Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test damage_cannot_be_prevented_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. Before the fix it reported `left: 2, right: 0` for the protected permanent and emitted no `DamageRedirected` receipt. The correction retains redirection but bypasses the target shield: the focused green trace is `DamageShieldCreated → DamageRedirected → DamageDealtToPlayer`, with no `DamagePrevented`. It also adds a bounded prospective-event choice pipeline: two live effects suspend one targeted direct-damage spell, the affected player submits an exact replacement identity, and `DamageReplacementApplied` precedes `DamagePrevented` or `DamageRedirected`. |
| `incarnation-receipt-breaks-library-search-audit` | Engine invariant-integration defect — high severity, every library search that finds a card | Fixed; red `8849581d`, green `170fc343` | After incarnation receipts were enabled, Civic Wayfinder moved its chosen library card, emitted `ObjectIncarnationAdvanced`, then emitted `LibrarySearchResolved`. The search audit required `CardMoved` immediately before the search receipt and rejected the valid action with `library-search receipt lacks movement to its declared destination`. Exact red command: `cargo run -q -p cardbench-magic-rav --bin rav-engine-parity`; it failed at `rav_civic_wayfinder_etb_basic_land_search action 4`. The audit now accepts exactly one matching structural incarnation receipt between its selected-card move and resolution receipt while preserving the immediate post-resolution shuffle requirement; `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test civic_wayfinder_search_red --quiet` passes. |
| `incarnation-receipt-breaks-activated-sacrifice-audit` | Engine invariant-integration defect — high severity, self-sacrifice and other activated sacrifice costs | Fixed; red `49bc3953`, green `f12fc79c` | After object-incarnation receipts and calculated activation costs were combined, a legal self-sacrifice activation wrote `SacrificedAsAbilityCost → CardMoved(Graveyard) → ObjectIncarnationAdvanced → AbilityActivated`. The sacrifice audit expected `CardMoved` immediately before `AbilityActivated`, stopped at the structural incarnation receipt, and rejected the otherwise valid transition with `IllegalAction("sacrifice-cost receipts do not match the activated ability binding")`. Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test activated_cost_modification_red tax_does_not_apply_to_mana_abilities_and_departure_revokes_it -- --exact --nocapture`. The invariant now steps over an incarnation receipt only when it is paired with its immediately preceding move, then consumes the original cost receipt and transition as one causal unit. The focused test passes and prints the complete taxed self-sacrifice lifecycle. |
| `general-named-counter-permanent-lifecycle-gap` | Engine counter-state defect — high severity, expansion-neutral permanent counters and later counter-removal effects | Fixed; red `0036b14c`, green `020beaba` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test general_counter_lifecycle_red -- --nocapture` failed with `left: None`, `right: Some(1)` after `TST-COUNTER-ENGINE` resolved its counter ability: the artifact had `counters: {}` and no `CounterPlaced` receipt. The green substrate introduces typed `CounterKind`, `CounterPlaced`/`CounterRemoved` receipts, typed add/remove source and permanent-target effects, positive/valid-kind and battlefield lifecycle invariants, atomic insufficient-removal rejection, and exact typed replacement-chain auditing. `general_counter_lifecycle_contract` prints `ReplacementEffectApplied(CounterPlacement { Charge }, 2→4) → CounterPlaced(Charge, 4)` and a later `CounterRemoved(Charge, 3)`; an attempted second removal reports `IllegalAction("counter removal requires sufficient counters")` while preserving the one remaining counter, live stack, and pre-resolution event log. Normal departure clears a named `quest` counter. Boundary: player/nonbattlefield counters and counter-removal activation costs remain unsupported. |
| `object-incarnation-zone-transition-and-source-provenance-gap` | Engine zone-change identity defect — critical severity, stack, source-relative effects, direct draw, and any later blink/linked-exile substrate | Fixed; red `784ab48d`, green `d09e0b0c` | Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test object_incarnation_zone_change_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` failed with `hand=1; stack=1; library=1; hand_after_draw=1` (the expected new incarnations were `2`) and later with `original=1; returned=3; characteristics power Some(2)`. The fix advances every real zone transition, including stack entry and draws, records `ObjectIncarnationAdvanced` immediately after the corresponding `CardMoved` or `SpellCast`, captures stack/effect/Aura endpoint incarnations, and makes source-relative effects no-op for an old source. It also keys activated/triggered lifecycle receipts by source incarnation; the green regression proves a dies trigger stacks and resolves with its old battlefield incarnation. Focused proof: the red test is now 3/3, `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --quiet` passes, and `cargo clippy -p cardbench-magic-engine --lib -- -D warnings` passes. RAV test sources compile; public scenario digests need their separate expected-event migration because the canonical stream now includes the new structural receipt. |
| `generic-trigger-scheduler-condition-decision-gap` | Engine trigger-scheduling defect — high severity, every non-attack trigger with targets, a targetless “may” decision, or a public discard/sacrifice selection | Fixed; red `41e4dedb`, green `163229ec` | The former card-shaped damage, dies, life-gain, ETB, land-entry, upkeep, attack, and cast queues now capture one `PendingTriggeredAbilityEvent` pipeline, materialize event-bound effects before placement, and place controller groups active-player-first. A target-bearing event remains off-stack until the existing controller target action succeeds; subsequent placements cannot overtake that choice. Optional triggers now suspend for an explicit accept/decline even with zero mana cost. One-effect all-player discard and controller-creature sacrifice triggers suspend at resolution for the actual chooser's `ChooseTriggeredAbilityEffectObject` submission, so neither selection uses stable fixture order. Exact green proof: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test belltower_sphinx_trigger_scheduler_red -- --nocapture` (2/2) prints `DamageDealtToPermanent → TriggeredAbilityStacked → ChooseTriggeredAbilityTargets → CardMoved(Graveyard) → AbilityResolved`; its target decision is controller-only and the stack is empty before submission. The Sadistic Augermage and Vindictive Mob contracts additionally prove each player chooses its own discard and the controller chooses the sacrificed creature. Remaining scope: controller ordering among multiple simultaneous triggers is stable binding/source order; a later policy ordering decision is still required for full CR 603.3b fidelity. |
| `apnap-simultaneous-trigger-ordering-missing` | Engine trigger-scheduler defect — high severity, every represented simultaneous group with multiple triggers controlled by one or more players | Fixed; red `2240fcbb`, green `4f92f529` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test apnap_trigger_ordering_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; it failed because two simultaneous same-controller triggers entered the stack in fixture order with no decision. The green slice adds public `TriggeredAbilityOrder` decisions with exact source/incarnation/ability options and full-permutation validation, then preserves existing active-player-first APNAP grouping. Event evidence: a land entry opens Player 0's `DecisionOpened(TriggeredAbilityOrder)`, then `DecisionCompleted → TriggeredAbilityOrderChosen([second, first]) → TriggeredAbilityStacked(second) → TriggeredAbilityStacked(first) → TriggeredAbilityStacked(nonactive)`. The no-priority boundary and receipt ordering are invariant-audited. Boundary: not every Oracle trigger condition or intervening-if predicate is represented yet; this orders the engine's existing simultaneous trigger batches. |
| `generic-mana-spend-provenance-missing` | Engine payment/stack-provenance defect — high severity, every conditional-on-spent-mana spell | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | `ManaPool::pay` deterministically drained generic mana by color order and left no player-selected allocation, stack provenance, or event receipt. The red contract `ribbons_spent_mana_red` required Ribbons to reject the ambiguous legacy cast rather than silently accept it; the prior engine returned `Ok(())` and wrote `SpellCast`. `Game::cast_spell_with_mana_spend` now validates exact post-Convoke generic/hybrid choices against the live pool, emits `SpellManaPaid` immediately before `SpellCast`, and retains the identical ordered colors on the stack object. The complete Ribbons contract exercises Blue and Red allocations, the distinct draw/no-draw traces, and malformed-selection rollback. |
| `multiple-blocker-declaration-unrepresentable` | Engine combat/state-machine defect — high severity, every ordinary creature combat and represented Trample card | Fixed; red `da2efeba`, green `b399799f`, later ordering/deathtouch completion `8c3b8a70` | The combat state stored one `ObjectId` blocker per attacker, so the active red command `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test multiple_blockers_combat_red -- --nocapture` failed 0/3: each legal ordered two-blocker assignment returned `IllegalAction("invalid blocker assignment")` and the declaration trace remained empty. A prior ledger row incorrectly claimed this repair had landed while all three regressions were ignored. The green model stores nonempty ordered blocker vectors, preserves global blocker uniqueness and evasion provenance, assigns damage from every live blocker, assigns a nontrample attacker's complete positive power, and allocates Trample lethal damage through every live blocker before one defender receipt. The focused test is now 3/3; neighboring single-block, Trample, first-strike, and combat-invariant contracts also pass. Attacker-submitted ordering and deathtouch are now covered by the later generic-decision repair; arbitrary legal damage-amount choices remain bounded. |
| `single-blocker-nontrample-damage-underassigned` | Engine combat damage-assignment defect — high severity, every blocked non-Trample attacker with one blocker | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | After the multi-block model landed, a legal 3/3 attacker blocked by a 2/2 recorded only lethal damage (2) on the blocker instead of the attacker's full assigned 3. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test deck_combat_invariants submitted_combat_moves_deal_damage_run_sbas_and_preserve_invariants -- --exact --nocapture`; failure asserted the missing `DamageDealtToPermanent { source: blocked_attacker, permanent: blocker, amount: 3 }`. The resolver now sends all attacker damage to a single blocker while retaining declaration-order lethal allocation for multiple blockers. |
| `cast-payment-choice-mana-fixture-gap` | Public RAV scenario-substrate defect — medium severity, every selectable definition-bound mana ability used during cost payment | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | `CastRequest` already retained `ManaAbilityActivation::chosen_color`, but the shown scenario grammar accepted only `source_label:ability_id`. The red milestone `cebc6fb` recorded `rav_birds_of_paradise_flying_cast_payment action 7: unknown RAV cast-payment mana ability \`produce-one-color:white\` for \`RAV-BIRDS-OF-PARADISE\``. The grammar now accepts an optional third explicit color field for definition-bound abilities, rejects a color on typed basic-land entries, and threads the parsed color into the atomic cast request. The green fixture’s ordered receipts include the selected white output before the spell reaches the stack. |
| `trample-combat-assignment-missing` | Engine combat-damage assignment defect — high severity, every represented Trample creature | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified, later multi-block ordering/deathtouch completion `8c3b8a70` | Once the engine represents `Keyword::Trample`, a blocked 5/5 trample attacker still assigned all five damage to a 2/2 blocker and no damage to its defender. The red repro `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test trample_combat_damage_red -- --nocapture` reported `left: 0`, `right: 1` for the required 3-damage player receipt. The single-block substrate now assigns only remaining lethal damage (including pre-marked damage) to the sole blocker, delivers positive excess once to the defender, and sends all positive damage to that defender if the blocker left combat; invalid declaration provenance is rejected. Multi-block attacker ordering and deathtouch are covered by the later generic-decision repair; combat prevention/replacement ordering and arbitrary damage allocation remain explicit gaps. |
| `fiery-conclusion-missing-additional-sacrifice-cost` | RAV card-fidelity / expansion-neutral cast-cost substrate defect — high severity, Fiery Conclusion and future sacrifice-cost spells | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | Fiery Conclusion's executable definition represented only its target-damage instruction, so a caster with `{1}{R}` and no controlled creature could cast it for free. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test fiery_conclusion_sacrifice_cost_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; the red milestone printed `Fiery Conclusion missing-sacrifice result: Ok(())` and wrote `SpellCast` despite no sacrifice-zone move. The bound cost substrate now requires one explicit controlled-creature selection, emits `SacrificedAsAdditionalSpellCost` then its zone move before `SpellCast`, and atomically restores the creature and receipt log after a later payment-activation rejection. |
| `skyknight-positive-manifest-contract-drift` | Public RAV contract integration defect — medium severity, positive-fidelity manifest | Fixed; regression verified | Skyknight Legionnaire was added to `RAV_FULL_FIDELITY_DEFINITION_IDS`, but the exact full-fidelity contract still declared a 33-entry expected array. The red workspace gate failed to compile with `can't compare [&str; 34] with [&str; 33]`; repro: `cargo test --workspace --quiet` from `varieties/magic`. The exact contract now contains the 34th ID, so every positive-manifest authority is checked together. |
| `haste-summoning-sickness-exception-missing` | Engine combat/mana-ability rules defect — high severity, every creature with Haste | Fixed; regression verified | The engine could represent `Keyword::Haste`, but declaration and definition-bound tap-cost mana ability legality compared `entered_turn >= turn` unconditionally. The red repro `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test haste_summoning_sickness_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` recorded `Err(IllegalAction("illegal attacker"))` for the same-turn attack and `Err(IllegalAction("a summoning-sick creature cannot pay a tap mana-ability cost"))` for its tap ability, with no events. Haste now permits only those two exceptions, records attack declaration provenance, exposes the legal attack through `CardView`, and preserves atomic rejection for a non-Haste creature. |
| `live-setup-tap-hook-rewrites-gameplay-state` | Engine fixture-transition defect — high severity, every tap-cost permanent | Fixed; regression verified | `set_tapped_for_setup` is documented as a scenario-construction hook, but it accepted a live game after a Mountain's mana ability had tapped it. That silently erased a paid tap cost without priority, a stack item, or an event receipt. It now rejects every started game before it examines or changes the permanent, preserving the tap cost and empty receipt sequence. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test live_setup_tap_mutation_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; the previous implementation reported `result=Ok(())`, `tapped=false`, and `events=[]`. |
| `live-add-card-injection-bypasses-transition-machine` | Engine setup/zone ownership defect — high severity, every live game | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | `Game::add_card` could create a new object in a live hand, library, battlefield, graveyard, or exile after `begin_game`, with no priority action or event receipt. The red repro returned `Ok(ObjectId(1))`, mutated the hand, and left only the existing turn-start events. `add_card` now rejects every started game before allocation; `put_on_battlefield` inherits the same setup boundary. Exact regression: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test live_add_card_injection_red -- --nocapture`. |
| `live-grant-mana-injection-bypasses-transition-machine` | Engine setup/mana ownership defect — high severity, every live game | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | `Game::grant_mana` could add floating mana after `begin_game`, without priority, a mana action, or a `ManaAdded` receipt. The red repro returned `Ok(())`, changed Blue from zero to one, and left only turn-start events. `grant_mana` now rejects every started game before pool mutation; live mana must use the priority/action or intrinsic ability paths. Exact regression: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test live_grant_mana_injection_red -- --nocapture`. |
| `constructor-accepts-invalid-catalog` | Engine constructor/invariant defect — high severity, all expansion manifests | Fixed; regression verified | `Game::new` formerly validated mana-cost syntax and duplicate IDs but returned `Ok(Game)` when its catalog already violated the public invariant contract (for example, a definition marked basic but lacking the land type). The constructor now runs the same complete invariant audit before returning a game, so every later transition begins from a valid catalog and seat state. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test catalog_constructor_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; it formerly printed `malformed catalog construction result: Ok(Game { ... card_types: {Artifact}, is_basic_land: true ... })`. |
| `damage-spell-targets-noncreature-permanent` | Engine target-legality defect — high severity, executable RAV direct-damage slice | Fixed; regression verified | `TargetRequirement::Any` accepted every battlefield permanent, so the executable Lightning Helix accepted an opponent's Plains and placed it on the stack as a legal target. That is not a legal damage target in this card slice (the engine has creatures, players, and no permanent-wide damage targeting semantics). The engine now represents the narrower player-or-creature target requirement and assigns it to Lightning Helix and Char. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test noncreature_damage_target_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; it formerly failed with `Lightning Helix accepted a land target; result=Ok(()); events=["SpellCast { player: PlayerId(0), card: ObjectId(1) }"]`. |
| `life-total-overflow-panics-during-resolution` | Engine numeric/event-atomicity defect — high severity, ordinary RAV life-gain resolution | Fixed; regression verified | With the caster at `i16::MAX`, a legal Lightning Helix-equivalent RAV effect resolved its three player damage, then the controller-life addition overflowed and panicked before `LifeGained`, `SpellResolved`, and the zone move. The life-state, public view, scenario, and policy-result representation now use wide signed `i64` values, while effect amounts widen before arithmetic; the ordinary RAV resolution now completes its receipt and zone lifecycle beyond the former ceiling. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test life_total_overflow_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; it formerly preserved only `[SpellCast, PriorityPassed(0), PriorityPassed(1), DamageDealtToPlayer(1, 3)]`. |
| `colored-mana-cost-overflow-underpays` | Engine mana-cost accounting / transaction defect — high severity, expansion-neutral casting and convoke | Fixed; regression verified | Colored requirements were accumulated in a per-color `u8` with saturating addition. A legal model definition with 256 red symbols therefore accepted a 255-red pool, spent it, and wrote `SpellCast` instead of rejecting atomically for missing mana. Colored requirements now count in `u16` and reject before any pool slot is debited when they exceed representable capacity. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test colored_mana_cost_overflow_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. |
| `continuous-pt-overflow-panics-mid-installation` | Engine continuous-layer arithmetic / transaction defect — high severity, public continuous-effect transition | Fixed; regression verified | `characteristics` formerly added power and toughness modifiers in `i16`. Installing a representable `i16::MAX` power modifier on a 1-power creature therefore panicked during the public post-installation SBA check after retaining the effect and writing `ContinuousEffectCreated`. Derived layer-seven values now use widened `i32` arithmetic while printed values and individual modifiers remain `i16`, so the completed public transition has a defined result. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test continuous_pt_overflow_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; it formerly panicked at `engine/src/game.rs:1003:81` after `["ContinuousEffectCreated { source: ObjectId(1), target: ObjectId(1), layer: PowerToughness }"]`. |
| `damage-accumulator-overflow-panics-mid-resolution` | Engine damage accounting / resolution-transaction defect — high severity, multi-effect direct-damage substrate | Fixed; regression verified | Marked permanent damage formerly used `i16` addition. Two individually representable damage effects (first `i16::MAX`, then `1`) targeting the same creature resolve before SBAs, so the second mark panicked after the stack card had been popped and the first damage receipt had been written. Runtime marked damage and damage-event amounts now use widened `i32` values, preserving the entire resolution receipt and allowing the later SBA fixed point to handle lethal damage. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test damage_accumulator_overflow_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; it formerly panicked at `engine/src/game.rs:2170:21` after `["SpellCast { player: PlayerId(0), card: ObjectId(1) }", "PriorityPassed { player: PlayerId(0) }", "PriorityPassed { player: PlayerId(1) }", "DamageDealtToPermanent { source: ObjectId(1), permanent: ObjectId(2), amount: 32767 }"]`. |
| `negative-damage-effect-leaves-invalid-transition` | Engine effect-definition validation / policy-transaction defect — high severity, direct-damage substrate | Fixed; regression verified | `Effect::DealDamage` formerly accepted a negative signed amount. A policy could cast that malformed executable definition; on resolution it wrote `DamageDealtToPermanent { amount: -1 }`, left marked damage at `-1`, then returned an invariant error only after state and event log changes. Cast preflight now rejects nonpositive executable damage and life-gain amounts before targets, mana, zones, stack, or events change. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test negative_damage_effect_transition_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; the old rejected pass left `damage=-1` and appended `["PriorityPassed { player: PlayerId(1) }", "DamageDealtToPermanent { source: ObjectId(1), permanent: ObjectId(2), amount: -1 }", "SpellResolved { card: ObjectId(1) }", "CardMoved { card: ObjectId(1), to: Graveyard }", "PolicyMoveSubmitted { player: PlayerId(1), policy: \"red-regression\", kind: PassPriority }"]`. |
| `stack-target-kind-invariant-gap` | Engine invariant / stack construction defect — high severity, public-state mutation audit | Fixed; regression verified | The stack audit checked target count and unseated player identities but did not require the target enum variant to conform to the spell's target requirement. The invariant now separately validates immutable cast-time target shape while the resolver retains dynamic legality/rules-counter behavior for a departed player or battlefield object. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test stack_target_shape_integrity_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; it formerly accepted a player-only instant rewritten to `Target::Spell`. |
| `stack-permanent-target-identity-not-validated` | Engine invariant / stack-target provenance defect — high severity, every creature/permanent target | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | The stack audit validated a permanent target's enum shape but did not require its `ObjectId` to identify an allocated object. A hostile fixture could rewrite a legal creature-targeting spell to `Target::Permanent(ObjectId(99))` and receive `Ok(())`, although no casting transition could ever select that target. The audit now rejects object zero and every ID outside the monotonic allocation range, while retaining dynamic resolution legality for a once-valid target that changed zones or left with its player. Exact repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test stack_permanent_target_identity_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. |
| `stack-target-incarnation-not-revalidated` | Engine zone-change / stack-target provenance defect — critical severity, every targeted permanent or card that leaves and re-enters | Fixed; red milestone `03cef962`; green fix `99c54c00` | A targeted growth spell named a battlefield creature, a response destroyed it, and a later response returned the same physical card to the battlefield before the growth resolved. The red implementation retained only `ObjectId`, treated the re-entered object as the original target, and created a stale `ContinuousEffectCreated` modifier. The engine now assigns every card object a monotonic incarnation and every engine-created stack target occurrence a matching captured receipt. Resolution compares both object identity and incarnation, so the original spell emits `SpellCounteredByRules` and the re-entered creature remains unmodified. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test stack_target_incarnation_red -- --nocapture`; the pre-fix trace reported `power: Some(2)` and a stale `ContinuousEffectCreated`, while the green trace reports `power: Some(1)` and `SpellCounteredByRules`. |
| `protection-from-color-targeting-not-rejected` | Engine core keyword/target-legality defect — high severity, every colored spell or ability targeting a protected permanent | Fixed; red `8003d8f8`, green `6af9b517` | A synthetic white creature with `Keyword::Protection(Color::Red)` still accepted a red direct-damage spell as a legal target. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test protection_targeting_red -- --nocapture`; the pre-fix implementation returned `Ok(())`, placed the red spell on the stack, and emitted `SpellCast`. The green source-aware target path now rejects colored spell, activated-ability, and trigger targets before mutation and rechecks protection at resolution; combat declarations reject either protected side of a matching-color block; and non-targeted red damage consumes the ordinary `DamagePrevented` receipt path. The focused regression covers all three consequences. |
| `damage-cannot-be-prevented-does-not-bypass-redirection` | Historical engine replacement-ordering finding | Superseded by `damage-cannot-be-prevented-incorrectly-bypasses-nonprevention-replacements` | The earlier interpretation behind `ea149e64` incorrectly treated redirection as prevention. The correction is protected by red `c2459d19` and green `c6476e92`: unpreventable damage bypasses prevention, but a non-prevention redirection still applies. |
| `protection-source-color-provenance-lost-after-sacrifice` | Engine core protection/source-provenance defect — high severity, colored characteristics granted to an activated source that leaves before resolution | Fixed; red `d54ff0b1`, green `e18200c7` | Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test protection_source_color_provenance_red -- --nocapture` produced `DamageDealtToPermanent { amount: 2 }` and `AbilityResolved` after the source's red `ContinuousEffectExpired` receipt. The fix records typed `StackObject::source_colors` at cast, activation, and trigger creation; target selection, resolution-time protection checks, and permanent-damage prevention use that snapshot. It validates color-shape and spell snapshot integrity, while preserving current-source behavior outside stack resolution. Green trace ends in `AbilityCounteredByRules { source: ObjectId(2), source_incarnation: 1, ability: "ping" }` with zero target damage. The focused suite also proves nonmatching/colorless damage remains unprevented and a matching-color Aura is put into its owner's graveyard by SBA with linked effects expired. Boundary: this is typed color protection only; equipment and non-color protection qualities remain unimplemented. |
| `stack-sorcery-timing-invariant-gap` | Engine invariant / stack construction defect — high severity, public-state mutation audit | Fixed; regression verified | The invariant audit accepted a noninstant stack object whose controller was not the active player. It now requires a noninstant to be the bottom stack object, controlled by the current active player, in a main phase; any later responses must be instants in this compatibility slice. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test stack_sorcery_timing_integrity_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. |
| `stack-departed-controller-invariant-gap` | Engine invariant / player-departure defect — high severity, multiplayer stack audit | Fixed; regression verified | In a continuing three-player game, the invariant audit accepted a stack object controlled by a player marked lost. Stack ownership cleanup removes these objects during legal player departure; the invariant now rejects a fabricated survivor-stack violation. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test stack_departed_controller_integrity_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. |
| `stack-unsupported-nonpermanent-invariant-gap` | Engine invariant / stack construction defect — medium severity, capability-boundary audit | Fixed; regression verified | `cast_spell` rejected an instant or sorcery with no executable front-face effect, but the invariant accepted the same definition when externally placed on the stack. It now fails closed for unsupported nonpermanent stack objects too. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test stack_unsupported_nonpermanent_integrity_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. |
| `active-player-departure-skips-pending-stack-work` | Engine multiplayer stack/turn-transition defect — high severity, CR 800.4i | Fixed; regression verified | In a legal three-player response stack, the top spell eliminated the active player while a survivor's lower response remained on the stack. The resolver formerly incremented the turn and emitted turn-two Untap/Upkeep before that response resolved. While the stack remains nonempty, the engine now retains the departed seat as current-turn identity, resets priority among survivors, and defers turn rollover until the remaining stack resolves. A later receipt-invariant hardening also regressed that legal transition: after `PlayerLost(0)` and `ObjectLeftGame(1, 0)`, the survivor's final pass returned `UnknownCard(ObjectId(1))` because the audit dereferenced the removed historical spell. The audit now accepts only a later matching `ObjectLeftGame` as that receipt's terminal path; arbitrary missing objects still fail. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test active_player_departure_stack_order_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; the original trace ended with premature `StepBegan(turn 2, ...)`. |
| `failed-stack-resolution-leaks-final-pass-and-pops-stack` | Engine stack-resolution transaction defect — critical severity, every fallible resolver path | Fixed; regression verified | `pass_priority` formerly wrote the final `PriorityPassed` receipt, reset pass state, and called a resolver that popped the authoritative top object before dispatching fallible effects. A malformed but shape-valid public fixture (`DealDamage` targeting a stack spell) then returned `IllegalTarget` while leaving the final-pass receipt and a shorter stack. Public priority transitions now run through an all-or-error in-memory checkpoint that validates a successful result and restores stack, zones, pass state, priority, turn state, and sealed event log on error. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test stack_resolution_atomicity_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. |
| `draw-loss-removes-resolving-source-before-terminal-zone` | Engine stack-resolution / player-departure transaction defect — high severity, every resolving draw effect that eliminates its controller | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | `DrawControllerIfManaColorSpent` can legally draw from an empty library. Its resolver records the controller's loss and invokes player-departure cleanup before `resolve_top_of_stack` records `SpellResolved` and decides the source card's destination. Cleanup removes the source object, so the later definition lookup returned `Err(UnknownCard(ObjectId(1)))`; the enclosing final priority pass rolled back the otherwise valid draw/loss sequence and left only the caster's first pass. The resolver now recognizes the source-departure terminal path after its instructions: `ObjectLeftGame` closes the visible `SpellCast` lifecycle, with no fabricated `SpellResolved` or zone-move receipt naming a removed object. Exact regression: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test stack_draw_loss_resolution_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. |
| `draw-loss-spell-effect-terminal-event-regression` | Engine stack-resolution / terminal lifecycle defect — high severity, any spell draw that eliminates its controller | Fixed; red milestone `cargo test --workspace --quiet`; green fix `e3a5042` | A later engine merge restored the source-departure path but stopped before recording `GameEnded`: `stack_draw_loss_resolution_red` returned `Err(IllegalAction("terminal game state and GameEnded lifecycle record disagree"))`, leaving the spell on the stack after `PlayerLost`/`ObjectLeftGame`. The spell-draw resolver now appends `GameEnded` after player-departure cleanup; the focused trace ends `PlayerLost`, `ObjectLeftGame`, `GameEnded` and the stack is empty. |
| `stack-spell-target-can-point-to-source-or-response` | Engine invariant / stack provenance defect — high severity, public-state mutation audit | Fixed; regression verified | The invariant confirmed that a `Target::Spell` named a current instant/sorcery but did not require that target to be lower on the stack than its source. A public fixture could therefore make a counterspell target itself (or a later response), a target identity that did not exist when it was cast. The invariant now rejects an extant spell target at the same or a higher stack position while allowing a formerly legal lower target that has already left the stack to be countered by the rules. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test stack_spell_target_order_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. |
| `departed-spell-target-kind-not-validated` | Engine invariant / stack-target provenance defect — high severity, every counterspell-style target | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | The invariant audit distinguished an extant spell target's stack order, but when `Target::Spell(card)` had already left the stack it checked only the enum variant. A public fixture could therefore mutate a legal counterspell target into a creature card in a graveyard and still receive `Ok(())`, although the card could never have been a legal instant-or-sorcery target at cast time. Exact red repro from `c46caa5`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test stack_departed_spell_target_kind_red -- --nocapture` printed `departed non-spell target audit: Ok(())` with the creature target and no resolution receipts. The fix validates the immutable card definition for both live and departed targets, retaining definitions when CR 800.4a removes a player's objects; the green contract also proves a formerly legal departed instant remains an allowed historical target. |
| `stack-collapses-independent-target-slots` | Engine stack/target-resolution defect — high severity, multi-target spell substrate and RAV Seeds of Strength | Fixed; regression verified | The stack model accepted exactly one target whenever any effect targeted, even when a spell had multiple independent target occurrences. The red probe's three-target modifier spell was rejected before it could enter the stack: `three-target cast result: Err(IllegalAction("the initial CardBench spell slice supports exactly one target")); stack: []; events: []`. Stack target slots now correspond one-for-one to target-bearing effects. At resolution each target is dynamically rechecked; all-illegal targets counter the spell, while legal target slots resolve and illegal slots do nothing. The public RAV parser now accepts `targets = [...]`, and Seeds of Strength records three independently selected slots. Exact regression: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test multi_target_stack_resolution_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` now reports the three target slots and passes after its middle target leaves the battlefield. |
| `stack-later-target-revalidation-rolls-back-resolution` | Engine target-revalidation/resolution defect — high severity, repeated counter target occurrences | Fixed; regression verified | Target legality is correctly snapshotted to decide whether a spell is countered by rules, but a later target-bearing instruction may become illegal because an earlier instruction resolved. A two-instruction counter response legally selects the same lower spell twice. The first instruction formerly removed that lower spell; the second still followed its stale `legal: true` plan and returned `Err(IllegalTarget(Spell(ObjectId(1))))`, so the outer priority transaction rolled back the otherwise valid complete resolution. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test stack_later_target_revalidation_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; the former trace retained both stack objects and only the pre-resolution receipts. The resolver now retains the initial all-targets-illegal decision but dynamically revalidates each instruction initially marked legal immediately before dispatch. The later occurrence emits `TargetInstructionSkipped`, then the response resolves and both objects move to their resulting zones. |
| `radiance-colorless-target-omits-target` | Engine rules/effect-selection defect — medium severity, expansion-neutral radiance scope | Fixed; regression verified | A colorless creature is a legal radiance target, but the shared-color filter selected no objects because its color set was empty. The spell resolved with the target's base power unchanged (`Some(2)`, expected `Some(4)`). Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test radiance_colorless_target_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. The expansion-neutral selector now includes the legal target directly, then selects other creatures by shared color. |
| `game-over-accepts-gameplay-action` | Engine defect | Fixed; regression verified | A `PlayLand` policy move succeeds after `PlayerLost`. |
| `game-over-allows-direct-draw-mutation` | Engine defect | Fixed; regression verified | `draw_card` moves a library card after game termination. |
| `opening-hand-event-overstates-cards-drawn` | Engine defect | Fixed; regression verified | A two-card library returns success for a seven-card opening hand and logs seven cards drawn. |
| `deck-validation-allows-sideboard-copy-limit-bypass` | Expansion/deck substrate defect | Fixed; regression verified | Five nonbasic copies in the sideboard pass a four-copy deck rule. |
| `multiplayer-elimination-prematurely-ends-game` | Engine defect | Fixed; regression verified | In a three-seat game, one elimination makes `is_game_over()` true with two survivors and no winner. |
| `eliminated-player-receives-priority` | Engine defect | Fixed; regression verified | The priority cycle assigns priority to an eliminated seat. |
| `transmute-accepts-instant-speed-activation` | Engine/card-rules defect | Fixed; regression verified | Muddle the Mixture's transmute action succeeds in response to a spell on the stack. |
| `dredge-accepts-out-of-window-activation` | Engine/card-rules defect | Fixed; regression verified | The direct public dredge operation changes zones without a pending draw to replace. |
| `unsupported-spell-front-face-resolves-as-noop` | Engine/card-coverage defect | Fixed; regression verified | Muddle the Mixture's counter face is executable, targets only a visible instant or sorcery spell on the stack, and emits a distinct counter receipt. |
| `combat-state-breaks-after-token-dies` | Engine invariant defect | Fixed; regression verified | A token blocker dies, is removed, and stale combat state then fails `validate_invariants()`. |
| `combat-view-breaks-after-token-dies` | Engine view/invariant defect | Fixed; regression verified | A token dies in combat, remains in historical combat state, and `GameView` dereferences its removed object. |
| `transmute-policy-view-hides-all-legal-search-targets` | Engine policy-view defect | Fixed; regression verified | A whole-deck policy can activate transmute only by naming a hidden library object ID, but its `GameView` exposes no legal candidate. |
| `deck-load-rejection-partially-mutates-library` | Engine setup-transaction defect | Fixed; regression verified | A deck with valid entries before an unknown card returns an error after placing the valid cards into the library. |
| `permanent-continuous-effect-outlives-source` | Engine invariant/effect-lifetime defect | Fixed; regression verified | State-based actions can kill a permanent-effect source while retaining its continuous effect, leaving a successful transition that fails the invariant audit. |
| `blocked-attacker-damages-player-after-blocker-leaves` | Engine combat-rules defect | Fixed; regression verified | A creature blocked before its blocker leaves combat is incorrectly treated as unblocked and deals player damage. |
| `zero-survivor-winner-query-panics` | Engine terminal-state defect | Fixed; regression verified | Simultaneous player losses leave no survivors and `Game::winner()` indexes an empty survivor list instead of returning no winner. |
| `step-marker-follows-automatic-work` | Event/state-machine defect | Fixed; regression verified | Draw, untap, and combat damage are emitted before the `StepBegan` event that must delimit them. |
| `setup-is-logged-after-turn-one-main` | Turn-start/event defect | Fixed; regression verified | A full-deck log begins at turn-one precombat main and only then loads decks and opening hands. |
| `untap-and-cleanup-grant-priority` | Engine turn-rules defect | Fixed; regression verified | Policies can pass, cast, or activate mana in Untap and ordinary Cleanup. |
| `first-draw-skipped-in-multiplayer` | Engine turn-rules defect | Fixed; regression verified | Player zero incorrectly skips the first draw in a three-player game. |
| `empty-combat-enters-blockers-and-damage` | Engine combat-rules defect | Fixed; regression verified | No declared attackers still reaches Declare Blockers and Combat Damage. |
| `pass-priority-creates-hidden-combat-declarations` | Engine transition/audit defect | Fixed; regression verified | A pass silently sets attacker/blocker declaration state without the turn-based action or its event. |
| `active-player-loss-deadlocks-combat` | Engine multiplayer-transition defect | Fixed; regression verified | A departed active player leaves a declaration step that no living player can legally perform. |
| `player-loss-leaves-owned-objects-in-game` | Engine multiplayer-zone defect | Fixed; regression verified | A player leaves a continuing multiplayer game but retains hand, library, battlefield, and stack objects. |
| `defender-departure-retargets-declared-attackers` | Engine multiplayer/combat defect — high severity, 3+ player combat target identity | Fixed; regression verified | After `PlayerId(0)` declared attackers against next-seat `PlayerId(1)`, a resolving spell eliminated `PlayerId(1)`. The engine then made `PlayerId(2)` declare blockers and assigned the already-declared attacker's combat damage to `PlayerId(2)`, instead of removing attackers from combat when their original defender departed. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test multiplayer_combat_defender_departure_red -- --nocapture`. Combat now retains the original defender, clears attackers when that seat leaves, and skips blocker/damage steps instead of retargeting. |
| `token-sba-has-no-terminal-lifecycle-event` | Event/zone-accounting defect | Fixed; regression verified | A token is removed by SBA without a `TokenCeasedToExist` receipt. |
| `transmute-shuffle-is-not-auditable` | Event/audit defect | Fixed; regression verified | Transmute changes a library's order but logs no `LibraryShuffled` receipt. |
| `terminal-game-has-no-end-event` | Event terminal-state defect | Fixed; regression verified | Logs have `PlayerLost` but no canonical terminal winner/draw event. |
| `game-ended-precedes-terminal-policy-receipt` | Event chronology defect | Fixed; regression verified | A passing policy move causes automatic loss, yet its acceptance receipt follows `GameEnded`. |
| `policy-cannot-choose-draw-replacement` | Policy/state-machine coverage defect | Fixed; regression verified | A full policy game could not choose Dredge at its draw-replacement boundary, so `Dredged` was absent from the event corpus despite direct API coverage. |
| `valid-draw-misclassified-as-matrix-failure` | Policy-harness result-classification defect | Fixed; regression verified | Two Char resolutions in the eight-seed corpus reduced both players to zero in one SBA pass; the engine correctly logged `GameEnded { winner: None }`, but the matrix treated the valid completed draw as a failure. |
| `reference-dimir-convoke-is-64-cards` | Deck-fixture defect | Fixed; regression verified | A declared 60-card probe contains 64 cards, weakening matrix comparability. |
| `pending-draw-replacement-allows-priority-interleaving` | Engine transition defect | Fixed; regression verified | While a mandatory Dredge-or-draw decision was pending, a policy could cast an instant, mutate the stack, and leave the stale replacement marker to be caught only later by invariants. Priority-bearing actions now reject atomically until `PolicyAction::Draw` resolves the decision. |
| `event-log-external-mutation-not-detected` | Engine audit defect | Fixed; regression verified | A caller could append, reorder, remove, or rewrite valid-looking public events and still pass the prior semantic event checks. The engine now seals its canonical event sequence and detects edits; `clear_event_log` is the explicit synchronized reset. |
| `public-seat-id-corruption-not-rejected` | Engine audit defect | Fixed; regression verified | Mutating player IDs or extending the public seat vector could evade the former indirect seat lookup. The audit now requires the original fixed seat count and exact `PlayerId(n)`/index correspondence. |
| `active-eliminated-seat-accepted-by-invariant-audit` | Engine audit defect | Fixed; regression verified | A continuing game whose active player had already lost could pass the former audit if priority had been reassigned. The invariant now rejects an eliminated active seat. |
| `zero-timestamp-continuous-effect-accepted` | Engine audit defect | Fixed; regression verified | A fabricated continuous effect with timestamp zero evaded the uniqueness/future-timestamp test. Effects now require positive timestamps as well as monotonicity. |
| `muddle-counterspell-front-face-unmodeled` | Engine/card-coverage defect | Fixed; regression verified | The prior honest fallback rejected Muddle's unsupported front face. The narrow executable RAV slice now targets an instant or sorcery card on the stack, removes it during resolution, and emits `SpellCountered`; its transmute behavior remains intact. |
| `negative-power-combat-damage-gains-life` | Engine combat/event defect | Fixed; regression verified | An unblocked creature reduced below zero power logged a negative `DamageDealtToPlayer` amount and increased the defending player's life; blocked creatures could likewise subtract marked damage. Nonpositive power now assigns no combat damage and emits no damage receipt. |
| `sorcery-cast-at-instant-speed` | Engine stack/timing defect | Fixed; regression verified | A nonactive player could cast a sorcery in response to an instant. The rejected cast is now atomic: the sorcery remains in hand and the stack and canonical event log are unchanged. |
| `casting-hands-priority-to-opponent` | Engine priority defect | Fixed; regression verified | Casting a spell immediately assigned priority to the next player. The caster now retains priority, the pass count resets, and an opponent's response is possible only after an auditable caster pass. |
| `public-mana-action-preserves-stale-pass-sequence` | Engine priority/event defect — high severity, public mana-action API | Fixed; regression verified | After `PlayerId(0)` passed in Upkeep, `PlayerId(1)` called `add_mana_from_action` and then passed. Because the mana action did not reset the prior pass, the engine advanced to Draw instead of returning priority to `PlayerId(0)`. The trace contains `PriorityPassed(0)`, `ManaAdded(1, Blue, 1)`, `PriorityPassed(1)`, then `StepBegan(Draw)`, skipping the response window. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test priority_mana_action_contract -- --nocapture`. The public mana-action seam now resets the pass sequence just like an intrinsic mana ability. |
