| `zero-amount-mana-action-reopens-priority` | Engine action-validity/event defect — medium severity, public mana-action API | Fixed; regression verified | After `PlayerId(0)` passes in Upkeep, `add_mana_from_action(PlayerId(1), Blue, 0)` returned success even though the pool remained zero. It emitted `ManaAdded { player: PlayerId(1), color: Blue, amount: 0 }` and reset the pass sequence, letting a caller manufacture a priority-consuming non-action. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test zero_amount_mana_action_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. The public seam now rejects zero amounts before mutating mana, event log, or pass state. |
| `weakness-report-preserves-stale-pass-sequence` | Engine priority/event defect — medium severity, policy reporting API | Fixed; regression verified | After `PlayerId(0)` passed in precombat main, `PlayerId(1)` submitted `ReportEngineWeakness` and then passed. The accepted non-pass report left the old pass count in place, so the engine advanced to Beginning of Combat instead of returning a response window to `PlayerId(0)`. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test report_priority_reset_red -- --nocapture` (trace: `PriorityPassed(0)`, `EngineWeaknessRevealed(1)`, `PolicyMoveSubmitted(ReportEngineWeakness)`, `PriorityPassed(1)`, `StepBegan(BeginningOfCombat)`). Reports now reset the pass sequence while remaining otherwise observational. |
| `stack-invariant-allows-fabricated-player-target` | Engine invariant/stack integrity defect — high severity, public-state mutation audit | Fixed; regression verified | A legal instant targeting `PlayerId(1)` was externally rewritten on the public stack to target unseated `PlayerId(99)`. `validate_invariants()` returned `Ok(())`, treating an impossible cast-time target as a plausible later-illegal target. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test stack_target_integrity_red -- --nocapture` (output: `fabricated stack-target audit result: Ok(()); ... targets: [Player(PlayerId(99))]`). The invariant now requires every stack player target to name a seated player while still allowing a later-lost seated target to be countered by the rules. |
| `setup-transitions-accepted-during-live-game` | Engine setup/turn-transition defect | Fixed; regression verified | A live game accepted `load_deck_into_library` for an empty seat and `draw_opening_hand` for a prepared seat, allowing hidden cards plus `DeckLoaded`, `LibraryShuffled`, or `OpeningHandDrawn` setup receipts to be injected mid-turn without priority. Both setup APIs now reject atomically after `begin_game`; a second opening hand also cannot be appended to a nonempty hand during setup. |
| `zone-change-silently-deletes-continuous-effect` | Engine event-log defect | Fixed; regression verified | When an effect source or target left the battlefield, the effect disappeared from state without `ContinuousEffectExpired`, leaving a creation-only lifecycle in canonical logs. Zone and player-departure cleanup now emits the expiration receipt after the corresponding move/leave event. |
| `public-continuous-effect-installation-skips-sbas` | Engine transition/SBA defect — high severity, public effect-installation API | Fixed; regression verified | Installing a legal permanent `ModifyPowerToughness { toughness: -1 }` effect on a one-toughness battlefield creature returns success but leaves it on the battlefield. The only receipt is `ContinuousEffectCreated`; no `StateBasedAction` or graveyard move follows, despite the completed transition having created a zero-toughness creature. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test continuous_effect_sba_public_transition_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` (trace: `zone=Some(Battlefield); events=["ContinuousEffectCreated { source: ObjectId(1), target: ObjectId(2), layer: PowerToughness }"]`). Public installation now reaches the SBA fixed point atomically, while an internal resolver primitive preserves the rule that SBAs wait until the entire spell finishes resolving. |
| `terminal-effect-installation-rejection-mutates-state` | Engine transaction/terminal-lifecycle defect — high severity, public effect-installation API | Fixed; regression verified | After `PlayerId(1)` lost and the terminal `GameEnded { winner: Some(PlayerId(0)) }` receipt was present, `add_continuous_effect` returned an error only after appending `ContinuousEffectCreated` and retaining the new effect. The rejected call therefore left the event log nonterminal and failed the invariant audit. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test terminal_continuous_effect_atomicity_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. The public transition now rejects terminal games before it installs effects, changes timestamps, or writes lifecycle receipts. |
| `terminal-begin-game-rejection-appends-turn-event` | Engine transaction/terminal-lifecycle defect — high severity, public start-game API | Fixed; regression verified | A fixture reached a valid terminal state through `check_state_based_actions`, with `PlayerLost(1)` followed by `GameEnded { winner: Some(0) }`. `begin_game()` then returned `Err(IllegalAction("terminal game must end with exactly one matching GameEnded event"))` only after changing the live-game state and appending `StepBegan { turn: 1, active_player: PlayerId(0), step: Untap }` after the terminal receipt. The rejected call left an invalid event chronology instead of remaining atomic. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test terminal_begin_game_atomicity_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. Start now rejects an already-terminal fixture before changing live-game fields or emitting a turn receipt. |
| `begin-game-skips-first-priority-sba-boundary` | Engine turn-start / state-based-action defect — high severity, every authored starting battlefield with an SBA condition | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | `begin_game` recorded Untap and advanced automatically to Upkeep but did not run state-based actions before exposing player zero's first priority window. A 1/0 creature placed on the battlefield during fixture setup survived at Upkeep with no `StateBasedAction` or graveyard move, even though no player may receive priority while it remains. Untap now reaches the SBA fixed point after its automatic work and before the automatic advance to Upkeep, so the first priority-bearing state is stable. Exact regression: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test begin_game_sba_boundary_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. |
| `shown-scenario-count-understates-corpus` | Public RAV fixture defect | Fixed; regression verified | The public corpus now contains 121 deterministic scenarios after the Sewerdreg Fear and Moroii Flying scenario additions. The shown index, parity runner, and internal deterministic-scenario test all assert 121. Earlier red repros were stale expected-count failures (`left: 93`, `right: 92`, later `left: 105`, `right: 102`); `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --lib -- --nocapture` now passes. |
| `torpid-moloch-compatibility-contract-stale` | Public RAV contract drift after a card promotion | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | The Torpid Moloch lane promoted the card from the bounded chassis into the full-fidelity manifest, but the older wave-one contract still expected only `colored-cost-casting`, `base-characteristics`, and `defender`. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test easy_creature_chassis_wave_one_contract easy_creature_wave_one_is_exactly_bounded_to_public_base_facts -- --exact --nocapture`; the contract now expects the full five-rule definition and passes. |
| `flame-kin-zealot-chassis-contract-stale` | Public RAV contract drift after a card promotion | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | After Flame-Kin Zealot moved from the bounded chassis into the positive manifest, `cargo test --workspace --quiet` failed in `easy_creature_chassis_wave_three_contract` with `left: ["full-rules-fidelity", "colored-cost-casting", "base-characteristics", "etb-team-pump-haste"]` versus the stale bounded expectation `["colored-cost-casting", "base-characteristics"]`. The contract now excludes Flame-Kin Zealot from that bounded matrix. |
| `thundersong-trumpeter-chassis-contract-stale` | Public RAV contract drift after a card promotion | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | Removing the already-promoted Flame-Kin Zealot from the wave-three bounded matrix exposed the next stale entry: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test easy_creature_chassis_wave_three_contract -- --nocapture` failed with `RAV-THUNDERSONG-TRUMPETER` carrying `full-rules-fidelity` and `tap-prevent-target-combat` instead of the bounded two-rule contract. The green fix `4812dba` removes promoted Flame and Trumpeter from that bounded matrix. |
| `typed-basic-land-cast-payment-unavailable` | Engine rules-transition defect — high severity, all typed basic lands | Fixed; regression verified | `CastRequest` originally admitted only definition-bound mana abilities. A public RAV player explicitly naming Forest and Plains while casting the colored Watchwolf chassis received `Err(IllegalAction("intrinsic basic-land mana abilities are unavailable while paying a spell cost"))`, even though both source/color choices were legal and the prior workaround required an artificial separate mana action. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test basic_land_cast_payment_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; the left/right failure was that exact `Err` versus `Ok(())`. The cast-payment sum now accepts only explicitly selected registered typed basic lands, validates fixed color, records context → intrinsic activation → mana output → spell receipts in order, and rolls back source taps, pool, stack, and event log after a later payment failure. |
| `direct-draw-bypasses-turn-state-machine` | Engine transition defect — high severity, public gameplay API | Fixed; regression verified | After `begin_game` reached `Upkeep`, public `draw_card(PlayerId(0), None)` succeeded even though no draw instruction or pending Draw-step replacement existed. It moved `ObjectId(1)` from library to hand and emitted `CardMoved` without a policy receipt. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test direct_draw_timing_contract -- --nocapture` (output: `card zone: Some(Hand); new events: ["CardMoved { card: ObjectId(1), to: Hand }"]`). Live draws now require the active player's pending Draw-step marker and clear it atomically. |
| `unstarted-fixture-draw-leaves-stale-replacement-marker` | Engine transition defect — medium severity, authored-fixture turn-state scope | Fixed; regression verified | A fixture can publicly advance to player one's pending draw before `begin_game`; `resolve_pending_draw(PlayerId(1), None)` moved the card to hand but left `draw_replacement_pending` true. The following priority pass was rejected as an unresolved mandatory decision. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test direct_draw_timing_contract pending_draw_resolution_clears_its_marker_in_an_unstarted_fixture_turn -- --exact --nocapture`. Any existing pending marker is now consumed atomically when the ordinary draw resolves; the `started` guard remains limited to rejecting arbitrary live draws. |
| `transmute-hands-priority-to-opponent` | Engine priority defect — medium severity, activated-ability and RAV-policy scope | Fixed; regression verified | A legal Transmute activation recorded its search/shuffle events then set priority to `PlayerId(1)` rather than the activating `PlayerId(0)`. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test turn_lifecycle_adversarial transmute_activation_keeps_priority_with_its_controller -- --exact --nocapture`; the trace ends with `Transmuted(0, 1, 2)`, followed by `left: PlayerId(1)`, `right: PlayerId(0)`. The supported atomic activation now retains its controller's priority. |
| `priority-actions-bypass-mandatory-combat-declarations` | Engine priority/combat transition defect — high severity, all instant/activated action scope | Fixed; regression verified | At `DeclareAttackers` before attackers were declared, and at `DeclareBlockers` before blockers were declared, `cast_spell` accepted an instant. Each red trace logged `StepBegan` for the declaration step immediately followed by `SpellCast`, even though the required turn-based declaration must happen before either player receives priority. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test stack_priority_turn_based_action_red -- --nocapture`. All ordinary priority actions now reject until the appropriate combat declaration completes. |
| `eliminated-player-leaks-through-policy-view` | Engine policy-view/multiplayer defect — high severity, all multiplayer target-selection scope | Fixed; regression verified | In a continuing three-player game after `PlayerId(1)` lost, `view_for_player(PlayerId(0)).opponent_life` still returned `[(PlayerId(1), 0), (PlayerId(2), 20)]`. A policy that selects the first opponent could submit an illegal target for the departed seat even though the engine correctly rejects it. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test multiplayer_view_elimination_red -- --nocapture`. The view now filters departed seats from opponent life and battlefield projections. |
| `muddle-counter-policy-skips-available-mana` | Policy coverage defect — medium severity, natural RAV counterspell exercise | Fixed; regression verified | With Muddle in hand, two untapped Islands, priority, and an opposing Char on the stack, `rav.dimir-transmute-helix.v1` returned `PassPriority` instead of activating Blue mana toward its available counterspell. The engine's targeted counter scenario still passed, but natural policy logs contained zero `SpellCountered` events. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-policies dimir_transmute_helix::tests::activates_available_blue_mana_before_countering_a_stack_spell -- --exact --nocapture`. The policy now activates exactly the needed available Blue mana before submitting Muddle, and its regression records `SpellCountered`. |
| `public-game-fields-can-bypass-transition-machine` | Engine API encapsulation weakness | Open; explicitly bounded; red `a7ad4134`, probe quarantined `53547583` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test state_provenance_boundary_red -- --nocapture` fails because external `game.players[0].life = 13` leaves the canonical event log unchanged while `validate_invariants()` returns `Ok(())` (`fabricated life transition: audit=Ok(()); life=13; events_unchanged=true`). A normal suite run skips this ignored adversarial probe so green tests remain meaningful. No narrow green fix is safe: privacy/accessor or state-seal migration spans approximately 1,982 direct field references, 74 `players` consumers, and 26 intentional raw fixture mutations. |
| `saturated-public-mana-action-logs-phantom-mana` | Engine mana/event-atomicity defect — high severity, public and intrinsic priority actions and audit receipts | Fixed; regressions verified | `add_mana_from_action` formerly used saturating pool addition but recorded the requested amount unconditionally. With a Red pool already at `u8::MAX`, a further Red action for `1` succeeded and wrote `ManaAdded { amount: 1 }` even though the observable pool remained `255`; the receipt therefore claimed mana that did not exist. The same unchecked saturation let an intrinsic land ability tap its source and write `ManaAbilityActivated` plus `ManaAdded` without increasing a full pool. Every direct mana producer now preflights pool capacity and rejects atomically before source, pass, pool, or event changes. Red repros: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test mana_action_capacity_atomicity_red -- --nocapture` and `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test intrinsic_mana_capacity_atomicity_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`. |
| `mana-pool-total-overflows-during-bound-activation` | Engine mana-payment/state-machine defect — high severity, bound mana abilities | Fixed; regression verified | A player may hold the maximum valid `u8` amount of Blue and Red mana through the public mana-action API, then activate a legal `{1}` paid mana bundle. Generic-cost payment formerly totaled the five `u8` pool entries with ordinary addition and panicked on the 510-mana intermediate sum, rather than completing the activation. The payment path now uses a widened `u16` total; the legacy policy-facing `u8` total is explicitly saturated and cannot decide payment affordability. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test bound_mana_total_overflow_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; its trace clears setup receipts before the panic so the recorded action list is empty. |
| `flying-blocker-legality-missing` | Engine combat/state-machine defect — high severity, flying declaration legality | Fixed; regression verified | The engine represented `Keyword::Flying` but its blocker declaration accepted an ordinary ground creature against a flying attacker. It therefore wrote a successful `BlockersDeclared` receipt and advanced through an illegal transition. Exact red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test flying_reach_blocking_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic`; it printed `result=Ok(())` and a `BlockersDeclared { player: PlayerId(1), assignments: [(ObjectId(1), ObjectId(2))] }` receipt. The engine now snapshots Flying attacker and Flying/Reach blocker qualification at their declarations, rejects the illegal assignment atomically, and audits that combat provenance. |

Severity for the newly fixed engine findings is high for
`negative-power-combat-damage-gains-life`, medium for
`setup-transitions-accepted-during-live-game` and
`zone-change-silently-deletes-continuous-effect`, and medium rules-conformance
severity for `sorcery-cast-at-instant-speed` and
`casting-hands-priority-to-opponent`.

| `damage-shield-cleanup-underflow` | Engine prevention-layer invariant defect — high severity, any consumed temporary damage shield | Fixed; red milestone `bfa4725`; green fix `8da1bd8` | A consumed `AddDamageShield` effect now clamps cleanup at zero, preserving the ordered `DamagePrevented` and remaining-damage receipts while preventing negative shield state. |
| `rav-engine-audit-runtime-bound` | Audit infrastructure limitation — high operational severity, post-hardening policy campaign | Fixed; bounded campaign verified | The matrix created one scoped OS thread for every ordered matchup/seed job: 15 decks × 14 opponents × 3 seeds created 630 simultaneous workers. `cargo run -q -p cardbench-magic-policies --bin rav-engine-audit` consequently produced no stdout or finding count after more than seven minutes and was interrupted. Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-policies --lib deck_match::tests::reference_matrix_caps_live_workers_instead_of_spawning_one_thread_per_match -- --exact --nocapture`; it failed to compile with `cannot find function reference_matrix_worker_limit`. The matrix now caps live workers at available parallelism and 16, retains deterministic job-index output, and the audit flushes stage/seed progress. The default interactive command completed all 210 ordered one-seed matches with `finding_count=0`; broader runs remain available through `RAV_AUDIT_SEED_COUNT`. |

## RAV set and mechanic coverage gaps (not engine defects)

| `belltower-sphinx-source-controller-mill` | RAV card-fidelity defect — the damage trigger incorrectly exposed a player target and milled an arbitrary chosen player instead of the damage source's controller | Fixed; red `c6cd9824`; green `1419dda2` | The red command failed to compile because the required source-controller effect did not exist: `no variant ... MillSourceControllerFromSourceDamage`. The green substrate captures the damage source controller before deferred trigger placement, materializes a target-free `MillCapturedPlayer` effect with the positive damage amount, and verifies `DamageDealtToPermanent → TriggeredAbilityStacked → CardMoved(Graveyard) → AbilityResolved`; the source controller's library changes while the Sphinx controller's library remains untouched. |
| `benevolent-ancestor-defender-omitted` | RAV card-coverage gap — static Defender was absent from the executable definition | Fixed; red-to-green regression verified | The bounded Benevolent Ancestor definition previously modeled only colored-cost casting and base characteristics. Red repro: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test benevolent_ancestor_defender_red -- --nocapture` failed with `left: []`, `right: [Defender]`. The definition now records Defender and its rejected attack declaration in `rav_benevolent_ancestor_defender_compatibility`; its damage-prevention activation remains outside the slice and has an ignored full-fidelity probe. |
| `frenzied-goblin-and-sell-sword-brute-trigger-substrate-missing` | Engine triggered-ability substrate / RAV card-fidelity gap — high severity, target-bearing attack and dies triggers plus optional triggered mana costs | Fixed for the exercised deterministic policy path; red milestone `fa7ff93`; green fix `c40e932` | Trigger bindings now support attack and dies conditions, target requirements, optional mana payment, source-specific target restriction, and dead-source stack objects. Focused scenarios cover the Goblin's paid attack trigger and the Brute's dies damage trigger. |
| `coalhauler-swine-damage-received-trigger-missing` | RAV card-coverage gap — a damaged permanent must create a source-specific stack trigger that distributes the received amount to each player | Fixed; red milestone `ce8dee8`; green fix `c40e932` | Recipient-damage dispatch captures positive damage before SBAs, materializes the amount into a player-only damage effect, and stacks Coalhauler's trigger. The focused event log shows `DamageDealtToPermanent(amount:1)`, then `TriggeredAbilityStacked("damage-each-player")`, then one damage to each player. |
| `searing-meditation-life-gain-trigger-missing` | RAV card-coverage gap — high severity, Searing Meditation's optional life-gain trigger, payment, target, and damage | Fixed; red milestone `03e60d01`; green fix `9c811b20` | The red probe failed both contracts because `RAV-SEARING-MEDITATION` was absent. The green slice adds the full enchantment definition, a LifeGained trigger with optional generic-two payment, dynamic player-or-creature targeting, and event-order coverage. Its deterministic path pays when available; policy-declared decline and target choice remain explicitly open. |
| `life-gain-trigger-controller-scope` | Engine trigger-routing defect — a controller's life gain queued opponent-controlled `LifeGained` triggers | Fixed; red `85b45347`, green `aa2d8031` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test searing_meditation_red searing_meditation_triggers_only_for_its_controller_life_gain -- --nocapture`; the old trace stacked `life-gain-deal-two` from `ObjectId(1)` and opponent-controlled `ObjectId(2)` after one player-zero Lightning Helix `LifeGained`. The fix threads the gaining `PlayerId` into life-gain trigger capture and filters sources by controller. The green trace has exactly one `TriggeredAbilityStacked`, sourced by player zero's enchantment; focused engine/RAV tests remain invariant-valid. |
| `frenzied-goblin-trigger-choice-not-policy-submitted` | RAV/engine fidelity gap — optional trigger payment and target choice were implicit | Fixed by the trigger policy-decision substrate | Frenzied Goblin's target is now submitted through `ChooseTriggeredAbilityTargets`, and its optional `{R}` is explicitly accepted or declined through `ResolveOptionalTriggeredAbility` after the response window. No stable-order target or affordability-based auto-payment remains. |
| `trigger-target-choice-bypasses-policy-boundary` | Core state-machine defect — target-bearing triggers mutate the stack before the controller chooses targets | Fixed; red `7e72a30f` | The red command `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test trigger_policy_decision_red -- --nocapture` failed because declaration immediately emitted `TriggeredAbilityStacked` and selected the first of two legal creatures. `PolicyAction::ChooseTriggeredAbilityTargets` now owns a no-priority pending boundary, projects ordered legal options only to the controller, rejects mismatched identities/targets atomically, and stacks the trigger only after a valid submission. |
| `searing-meditation-trigger-choice-not-policy-submitted` | RAV/engine fidelity gap — optional trigger payment and target choice were implicit | Fixed by the trigger policy-decision substrate | Searing Meditation now suspends after all priority passes. Its controller explicitly accepts/declines `{2}` and, on acceptance, submits the conditional legal player-or-creature target. Decline spends no mana and requires no target. |
| `optional-trigger-cost-auto-paid-at-resolution` | Core state-machine defect — resolving optional triggered costs bypassed policy submission | Fixed; red `d82024df` | The exact red command emitted `AbilityManaPaid`, dealt two, and emptied the stack immediately after the second pass. Resolution now leaves the trigger on the stack at a no-priority decision boundary. The controller-only view reports cost, affordability, and conditional legal targets; a matching policy submission pays and resolves or declines without payment/effect. |
| `additional-red-creature-coverage-missing` | RAV card-coverage gap — Sparkmage Apprentice, Hunted Dragon, and Razia creature abilities/keywords | Fixed; red milestone `82b2166`; green fix `746163a` (strict-Clippy follow-up `f6e77e6`) | The red probe `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test additional_red_creatures_red -- --nocapture` failed because all three definitions were absent. The green slice adds Sparkmage's targeted ETB damage, Hunted Dragon's one-targeted-opponent ETB with three 2/2 first-strike Knight tokens, and Razia's Flying/Vigilance/Haste plus its two-target tap redirection ability. The multiplayer target regression is explicit: Hunted Dragon's trigger stack object retains exactly one opponent target rather than creating tokens for every opponent. |
| `screeching-griffin-source-blocker-restriction-missing` | RAV card-coverage gap — source-relative activated blocking restriction | Fixed; red milestone `9f73e85`; green fix `36f96ae` | The red probe found the Griffin's full-fidelity definition missing. The green slice binds `{R}: target creature can't block this turn` with a source-relative target restriction, preserving Flying and allowing unrelated blockers. |
| `surge-of-zeal-radiance-haste-missing` | RAV card-coverage gap — Radiance temporary Haste grant | Fixed; red milestone `9518003`; green fix `2c6ff9c` | The red probe failed at `Surge of Zeal definition exists`; the green slice adds the full instant definition and resolution-time Radiance Haste grant to the target and creatures sharing its color. |
| `frenzied-goblin-overbroad-combat-restriction` | RAV card-fidelity defect — attack-trigger restriction scope | Fixed; red milestone `9be29c7`; green fix `8518eeae` | The attack trigger incorrectly installed `CannotAttackOrBlock`, preventing the selected creature from attacking as well as blocking. The typed `CannotBlock` keyword now preserves attack eligibility while blocker declaration rejects the creature; Thundersong's all-combat `CannotAttackOrBlock` behavior remains distinct. |
| `hammerfist-giant-global-damage-missing` | RAV card-coverage gap — high severity, tap ability and non-Flying global damage | Fixed; red milestone `1b4aa7e`; green fix `43836bc` | The red probe failed because Hammerfist Giant was not executable. The green slice adds the exact `{4}{R}{R}` 5/4 definition, a typed tap ability, and a snapshot of non-Flying creatures only before state-based actions; the focused test records `DamageDealtToPermanent` for ground creatures and no damage for a Flying creature. |
| `seismic-spike-land-and-mana-effect-missing` | RAV card-coverage gap — high severity, targeted land destruction plus red-mana effect | Fixed; red milestone `e073694`; green fix `cf965605` | The red probe failed at `Seismic Spike definition exists` (0 passed, 1 failed). The green slice adds the `{3}{R}` sorcery, target-land legality, ordered `CardDestroyed` then `ManaAdded { color: Red, amount: 2 }` receipts, and a post-resolution invariant check proving the land is in the graveyard and exactly two red mana remain. |
| `rav-executable-count-overstates-catalog` | Public RAV catalog-contract defect — medium severity, executable boundary accounting | Fixed; red milestone `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --tests --quiet`; green fix `catalog_coverage` count reconciliation | The promoted-card contract expected 148 executable printings, but the authoritative catalog contained 145 (`assertion left: 145 right: 148` in `catalog_coverage::executable_slice_size_is_explicit_and_does_not_masquerade_as_set_coverage`). The count contract now matches the catalog at 151 printings / 136 unique names / 155 catalog-only names after Devouring Light, and the independent coverage report remains auditable. |
| `rav-coverage-report-contract-stale` | Public RAV coverage-report contract defect — medium severity, category counts lag promoted manifest | Fixed; red milestone `ae49faa`; green fix `45f3032` plus `99ceff2` | After successive promotions, the report contract lagged the manifest. It now asserts 69 full / 67 partial / 155 catalog-only unique names and 84 / 67 / 155 printings. |
| `rav-positive-manifest-length-understates-list` | Public RAV manifest contract defect — high severity, compile-time positive-definition boundary | Fixed; red milestone `abda5ff`; green fix `97123d9` | After Seismic Spike and Smash were promoted, `RAV_FULL_FIDELITY_DEFINITION_IDS` declared length 65 but contained 66 IDs. The crate failed to compile with `expected an array with a size of 65, found one with a size of 66`; the manifest now declares 66. |
| `rav-positive-manifest-exact-list-stale` | Public RAV manifest contract defect — medium severity, exact positive-definition list | Fixed; red milestone `b88d506`; green fix `97123d9` | After the manifest length was reconciled to 66, the exact-list contract still declared a 65-entry expected array and failed to compile with `can't compare [\&str; 66] with [\&str; 65]`. The expected Seismic Spike ID is now included in the exact list. |

The complete public inventory contains 306 printings / 291 unique names; the
executable compatibility slice contains 165 printings / 150 unique names.
Twenty executable printings are the four printings of each of five basic lands;
the other 145 executable names are nonbasic cards. Eighty-five of those
nonbasic cards plus all five typed basic lands are in the positive full-fidelity
manifest (90 total); the remaining 60 nonbasic cards are deliberately bounded
compatibility cards exercising generic casting, base characteristics, mana
abilities (including source-aware controller damage), targeted/global damage,
temporary modifiers, token creation, Convoke, Dredge, Radiance, Transmute,
combat-count damage, controller-wide temporary modifiers, hybrid payment,
typed basic-land lines, and first-strike combat damage. The remaining 141 unique names
are explicitly catalog-only and fail closed with
`card-specific-rules-not-implemented`. This boundary is enforced by
`catalog_coverage::executable_slice_size_is_explicit_and_does_not_masquerade_as_set_coverage`.

The four advertised RAV mechanics have targeted compatibility examples
(Convoke, Dredge, Radiance, and Transmute), but those examples do not establish
set-wide mechanic or card-text fidelity. In particular, the executable
Brownscale definition claims only its draw-replacement/base-characteristic
slice, Muddle claims only its narrow counter/transmute slice, and the engine
does not infer any semantics for the other cataloged cards. The 127 public
scenarios are behavioral probes for the implemented slice, not coverage of all
291 names or all interactions among the four mechanics.

## Non-engine result retained for policy work

`policy-matrix-incomplete-run` is not classified as an engine defect. The
`rav_boros_char_control` versus `rav_selesnya_convoke` pairing reached the old
80-turn development bound for shuffle seed 4 without an invariant failure. The
bound is now 120 turns, which permits the normal empty-library end condition in
a sixty-card two-player game; the 64-match policy matrix now completes with no
finding. It remains fail-closed if a future pairing reaches its bound.

## Corrective-loop rule

The audit is regression infrastructure, not a one-time report. Fixes are made
as a batch only after a discovery ledger is captured, then the same audit,
policy matrix, workspace tests, and Harbor verification are rerun. A new
finding reopens discovery before claims of a clean engine run.

## Regression evidence

After the initial corrective batch, the repeated audit completed with
`finding_count=0` over its public API probes and 64 shuffled policy matches
(four deck/policy pairings × 16 seeds). The original 16-seed fail-closed
tournament reported `failure_count=0`; `cargo test --workspace`, strict
Clippy, and Harbor's public 11-scenario engine verifier also passed.

## Expansion-round evidence

The broader six-deck policy matrix discovered and fixed the token-combat view
defect above. Its repeated public campaign ran 90 ordered full-deck games
(six fixtures × five opponents × three deterministic seeds) with
`finding_count=0`. That historical campaign is retained as baseline evidence;
the current fifteen-deck corpus is recorded with its own canonical event-log
manifest rather than overwriting that result.

## Invariant-expansion evidence

The invariant expansion now includes twelve broad public-API contract tests,
seven event-log contracts, five turn-state-machine contracts, seven zone/SBA
contracts, and three deterministic stateful property tests. The property tests
execute 64 three-player traces, each with 199 accepted and 33 intentionally
rejected policy actions, checking the invariant audit and every player view
after every attempt. Rules review of the initial 210-log corpus surfaced the
fixed turn, event, token, and multiplayer-transition defects above; the new
tests make those transitions fail closed rather than merely documenting them.
The draw-replacement ABI is additionally exercised through a real three-player
policy submission: its canonical trace contains `Dredged` followed by a
`PolicyMoveSubmitted { kind: Draw }` receipt. The open public-field boundary
remains a deliberate limitation of the fixture-oriented API, not a waived
invariant failure.

## Transition-hardening evidence

The follow-up adversarial tranche adds four public-state mutation tests (18
deliberate corruptions), four draw-replacement/multiplayer tests, two RAV
stack-target tests, and a fixed-digest public Muddle counterspell scenario. It
specifically exercises visible-stack countering, response LIFO order,
all-targets-illegal rules counters, mandatory replacement decisions,
three-player elimination handoff, and combat elimination. The engine suite
contains 53 tests, including a 64-seed stateful policy campaign; all pass after
the fixes above. The public field API remains intentionally
fixture-oriented, so a shape-valid externally fabricated state remains the
open provenance boundary rather than a claim that every state arose through a
legal transition.

The fresh post-hardening replay is retained at
`artifacts/rav-reference-deck-matrix/round7-transition-hardening/` (ignored
run output): 1,680 ordered games, 1,213,213 accepted moves, and 3,417,010
events. It has `failure_count=0`, 794 seat-zero wins, 884 seat-one wins, and
two valid draws. The manifest contains exactly 1,680 uniquely named logs;
manual review found zero event-count/header mismatches, zero unexpected
termination values, and a terminal `GameEnded` receipt in every trace.
Representative logs confirmed the ordered Dredge and Transmute receipts and
the simultaneous-loss cleanup ordering.

This natural policy corpus has 232 `Dredged` and 773 `Transmuted` events but
zero `SpellCountered` and zero `SpellCounteredByRules` events. That is a
coverage observation, not a clean bill of health for either counter path. The
persisted `rav_muddle_counterspell` scenario and RAV stack-target contracts
therefore remain mandatory targeted evidence for countering, LIFO response
resolution, and rules-based target failure.

After the Muddle-response policy correction, a fresh exported one-seed matrix
ran 210 ordered games with `failure_count=0` and a complete 210-log manifest.
Manual event-log review still found zero natural `SpellCountered` receipts.
The new policy regression proves the policy activates two available Islands,
casts Muddle, and records `SpellCountered` in its controlled response window;
the zero-count matrix result remains a coverage gap rather than evidence that
the counter path is broadly exercised by stochastic full-deck play.

## Eight-seed event-log review

After the draw-replacement and valid-draw corrections, the full public matrix
ran all fifteen ordered-deck policies against one another for seeds 0 through
7: 1,680 games, 1,213,213 accepted policy moves, and 3,417,010 logged
events. It completed with `failure_count=0`; 794 games were won by seat zero,
884 by seat one, and two were rules-valid simultaneous-loss draws. The corpus
contains 232 `Dredged` events in 102 games, 773 `Transmuted` events, 2,315
`TokenCeasedToExist` events, and 1,509 `ContinuousEffectExpired` events.

Manifest/header counts matched every log, every trace ended in exactly one
`GameEnded` record, and aggregate event checks found zero priority passes in
automatic Untap/Cleanup steps and zero empty-attacker combats that entered
blockers or damage. The corpus did not naturally produce
`SpellCounteredByRules` or `EngineWeaknessRevealed`; those are retained as
targeted public invariant/reporting tests rather than misrepresented as
whole-deck coverage.

Green commit `de398a93` adds `{B}{B}` 7/7 Trample, its target-opponent ETB stack binding, two green 3/3 Centaurs, a 123rd public scenario (`rav_hunted_horror_centaur_compatibility`, `fnv1a64:bee1157cb84b6e57`), and an event trace containing `SpellCast`, `SpellResolved`, `TriggeredAbilityStacked`, two `TokenCreated`, and `AbilityResolved`. The green port adds the typed `{W}` 1/1 definition and a generic `AddTargetDamageShieldUntilEndOfTurn` replacement shield. The source is sacrificed before `AbilityActivated`; the shield resolves through the stack even though its source is in the graveyard, accepts a player or creature target, emits `DamageShieldCreated`, consumes only the next damage, records `DamagePrevented`, and expires or cleans up on the target/turn boundary. The focused Char trace ends with a valid invariant audit. |
| `boros-fury-shield-conditional-combat-prevention-not-reconciled` | RAV White/core combat-prevention gap — Boros Fury-Shield is catalog-only and cannot prevent all combat damage from one targeted combat creature while conditionally dealing damage from a red mana-spend receipt | Fixed; RAV red `e0fcf259`, core red `15cd7c37`, core green `8d035a1a`, RAV green `be1ef5d1` | Exact RAV red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test boros_fury_shield_red -- --nocapture` failed both `Boros Fury-Shield definition exists` and `UnknownDefinition("RAV-BOROS-FURY-SHIELD")`. | `radiance-damage-shield-effect-missing` | Core rules gap — existing Radiance instructions can damage, modify, untap, grant a keyword, or destroy enchantments, but cannot snapshot the selected shared-color creature set and install independent target-side one-shot damage shields. | Fixed; core red `ac7a5299`, core green `203638f8` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test radiance_damage_shield_red -- --nocapture` initially failed with `E0599: no variant named RadianceAddTargetDamageShieldUntilEndOfTurn found for enum Effect`. | `static-friendly-source-prevention-missing` | Engine static-continuous/replacement gap — no controller-wide creature characteristic can prevent damage only from sources controlled by that creature's controller | Fixed; red `7ed05904`, green `3ebc1347` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test static_friendly_source_prevention_red -- --nocapture` failed because `ControlledCreaturesAddKeyword` and `PreventDamageFromControlledSources` did not exist. The general static substrate supports any permanent source that grants this target-side quality to its controller's live creatures. Prospective permanent damage checks the source and recipient controllers, retains the existing color/prevention path, and derives/revokes the quality from the live source rather than storing a mutable shield. The focused two-controller trace records `DamagePrevented` only for the friendly source, then `DamageDealtToPermanent` for the opponent source, and is invariant-valid. |
| `conditional-target-combat-damage-prevention-missing` | Engine combat/replacement gap — no reusable one-target effect can prevent every combat-damage packet from the exact target creature through end of turn while observing an explicit mana-spend condition | Fixed; red `15cd7c37`, green `8d035a1a` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test conditional_combat_damage_prevention_red -- --nocapture` failed to compile because the conditional one-target effect and `CombatDamagePreventionCreated`/`CombatDamagePrevented` receipts did not exist. The substrate now uses an independent id-bearing record tied to one live creature incarnation and cleanup expiry; its typed target is `AttackingOrBlockingCreature`, so it never introduces a second target word. The synthetic red-aware trace uses ordinary mana abilities, priority, declare attackers, stack resolution, blockers, and combat damage; it records the creation, controller damage, and prevention receipt and ends invariant-valid. `DamageCannotBePrevented` bypasses this prevention record, while ordinary redirection remains separate. |
| `chant-of-vitu-ghazi-dynamic-life-gain-not-reconciled` | RAV White/Convoke coverage gap — Chant of Vitu-Ghazi was catalog-only and lacked its battlefield-creature-count life-gain effect | Fixed; red `a27da6c0`, green `7f65046a` | Exact red reproduction: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test chant_of_vitu_ghazi_red -- --nocapture` failed at `Chant of Vitu-Ghazi definition exists` before any game transition. | `activated-cost-modifier-pipeline-missing` | Engine activation-cost/state-machine defect — high severity, every nonmana activated ability with a live cost increase or reduction | Fixed; red `f3a2310e`, green `e0a6f1af` | Exact red reproduction: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test activated_cost_modification_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` initially allowed two live `{2}` generic modifiers to leave a `{1}{R}` activation payable with `{4}{R}`, and let an underfunded taxed activation discard and sacrifice before returning `Ok(())`. The generic green substrate adds immutable declarative `ActivatedAbilityCostModifierBinding`s, a typed `ActivatedAbilityCostContext`, explicit effective-cost payment selection, and `ActivatedAbilityCostCalculated` provenance immediately before `AbilityManaPaid`. It snapshots every live source/incarnation, applies increases before reductions, preserves colored/hybrid symbols, taxes only represented nonmana abilities, and recomputes from the battlefield so a departed source ceases to apply. Focused green trace: `ActivatedAbilityCostCalculated(base={1}{R}, increases=[{2}], reductions=[{1}], effective={2}{R}) → AbilityManaPaid({2}{R}) → AbilityActivated`; the source-departure trace pays a taxer's calculated `{2}`, records its sacrifice, then charges a later `{1}{R}` ability only its base cost. The 6-contract test also proves a failed effective payment leaves source and discarded card in their original zones with no receipts, and rejects out-of-bound mana-ability modifiers. Verified: focused 6/6, engine package tests, strict engine-library Clippy, format/diff checks, and RAV crate check pass. Scope boundary: generic-symbol increases/reductions for stack-using activated abilities only; mana-ability cost modification, alternative/nonmana-cost modification, and card-level Suppression Field promotion remain intentionally unimplemented. |
| `general-attachment-lifecycle-missing` | Engine attachment/state-machine gap — high severity, Equipment attach actions and generic attached-permanent lifecycle | Fixed; red `66415068`, green `01c80b25` | Exact red reproduction: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test general_attachment_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` failed `equipment_attach_ability_is_a_valid_generic_activated_ability` with `IllegalAction("aura attachment effects are valid only on permanent spells")`. The green substrate registers immutable typed `AttachmentBinding`s for Aura or Equipment sources, retaining ordinary typed stack target effects while making lifecycle data explicit. Equipment attach abilities now resolve through the normal stack, reattach atomically, expire only the prior linked effects, and retain an unattached battlefield permanent when its target departs or becomes illegal. Source departure expires linked effects; `EquipmentAttached` and `AttachmentDetached` receipts are replay-audited. Aura spells preserve compatibility, and `enter_attachment_without_cast` reuses the same typed restriction, protection, controller, endpoint-incarnation, and linked-effect checks atomically. Focused evidence: `general_attachment_red` is 3/3, including reattachment, source/target departure, and controller-relative no-cast Aura entry; linked-exile, control, protection, copy, and strict engine-library Clippy remain green. Boundary: no general card effect yet searches for or puts an arbitrary Aura/Equipment into play, equipment is not exposed as a policy action beyond a set-bound activated ability, and multi-attachment/fortification rules remain out of scope. |
| `transmute-immediate-hidden-search` | Engine casting/decision defect — high severity, Transmute was modelled as an immediate hand-zone operation that accepted a preselected hidden-library card | Fixed; red `8cd3f49f`, green `11784278` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test transmute_stack_boundary_red -- --nocapture` from `varieties/magic` failed `assertion left == right: Transmute must wait on the stack; left: 0; right: 1`. The green path charges the typed `{1}{U}{U}` cost and records `AbilityManaPaid → DiscardedAsAbilityCost → CardMoved(Graveyard) → AbilityActivated`, retaining a source-incarnation-backed `transmute` ability on the normal stack. After every player passes it opens a controller-private id-bearing `LibrarySearch` decision; the opponent sees no decision or candidates, ordinary priority is blocked, and a legal selection records `CardRevealed → CardMoved(Hand) → LibrarySearchResolved → LibraryShuffled → Transmuted → AbilityResolved`. The focused regression, 64-seed stateful policy campaign (including `SubmitDecision`), three Dimir policy contracts, engine package tests, strict engine-library Clippy, and format/diff checks pass. Scope boundary: frozen direct `Game::transmute(..., found)` calls remain an explicit scenario-compatibility helper; all policy-submitted Transmute uses the stack-backed path. |
| `spell-copy-may-retarget-is-forced` | Engine optional-choice defect — high severity, every represented effect whose copy instruction says the controller may choose new targets | Fixed; red `217170fb`, green `92c65eea` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test spell_copy_may_decline_red -- --nocapture`. It failed `retargeting is optional` with `left: 1`, `right: 0`: the decision forced exactly one new target, so a controller could not retain the copied spell's original target. The green continuation now requires `min=0` and `max=original target-slot count`; its empty `DecisionSelection::Targets` branch retains the original target values *and captured target incarnations*, while a nonempty answer must replace every target occurrence. Focused new and prior copy contracts, engine-library tests, and strict engine Clippy pass. |
| `spell-copy-effect-created-casting-permission-missing` | Engine stack/casting-permission gap — high severity, effects that create an additional spell or let a specific exiled card be cast | Fixed; red `ae0a616f`, green `015491a4` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test spell_copy_cast_permission_red -- --nocapture`; before the substrate existed it failed to compile because the copy effect, exile permission effect, unified target-choice continuation, and lifecycle events were absent. The repair gives an original spell a fresh virtual stack-copy identity, preserves its target provenance or opens a public id-bearing retarget decision, emits `SpellCopied` and a copy terminal receipt, and never puts the virtual object in a zone. It also grants a specific exiled card a current-turn, source/incarnation-backed standard-paid or no-cost permission with an explicit timing mode; successful use emits `SpellCastFromPermission` and ordinary stack lifecycle receipts. Focused evidence is 3/3: a copy retargets through `DecisionId`, a retained-target copy resolves without a decision, and an exiled sorcery casts at instant timing after a legal mana ability. Boundary: copy-of-copy, broad copy modifications/replacements, and non-exile permission effects remain explicit future work. |
| `typed-multicard-library-search-reveal-reorder-missing` | Engine hidden-information/decision gap — high severity, multi-card searches and effects that reveal then reorder a library slice | Fixed; red `29f66e49`, green `83e8209b` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test search_reveal_reorder_red -- --nocapture`. It initially failed to compile because `LibrarySearchCardinality`, `LibrarySearchRequirement::CardTypes`, `Effect::SearchControllerLibraryMany`, `Effect::RevealTopLibraryCardsAndReorder`, `DecisionKind::LibraryReorder`, `GameEvent::LibrarySearchBatchResolved`, and `GameEvent::LibraryReordered` did not exist. The repair adds typed all-of-card-type predicates, private stale-safe batch selections with `ZeroOrMore`/`Exactly` cardinality and legal fail-to-find behavior, batch movement/reveal/shuffle receipts, and a public top-N reveal/reorder continuation whose submitted top-to-bottom permutation is auditable before terminal stack resolution. Focused green evidence is 2/2: a zero-to-two creature search rejects a three-card answer atomically, privately moves two selected cards, and records `LibrarySearchBatchResolved → LibraryShuffled`; a top-three reveal projects the same decision to both players, records three `CardRevealed` entries, preserves the submitted order, then records `LibraryReordered → SpellResolved`. The receipt invariant rejects duplicate batch selections, missing ordered moves or immediate shuffle, and reorder entries without prior public reveals. Boundary: this does not yet model multiple independently sequenced search/reorder instructions in one resolver, bottom-placement, arbitrary library-shuffle choices, or richer predicates such as names, colors, supertypes, and relative ordering. |
| `trigger-target-choice-bypasses-generic-decision` | Engine state-machine/decision defect — high severity, target-bearing triggers were held in a parallel anonymous queue, outside the id-bearing `PendingDecision` lifecycle | Fixed; red `ed516ec4` and `81ecb499`, green `b23b9a26` | Exact red commands from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test generic_trigger_target_decision_red -- --nocapture` and `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test repeated_trigger_target_slots_red -- --nocapture`. The repair removes the anonymous queue, adds `DecisionKind::TriggeredAbilityTargets` and captured typed continuation provenance, makes the legacy action a checked shim over the live id, and permits repeated legal target selections only for non-distinct slots. Green traces record `AttackersDeclared → DecisionOpened → DecisionCompleted → TriggeredAbilityStacked`; stale id reuse is rejected atomically. This is a core engine fix only and changes no RAV catalog or card-fidelity count. |

| `chosen-x-controller-damage-shield-missing` | Engine chosen-X/damage-replacement gap — an X-valued spell cannot create a controller-targeted prevention shield that remains after its source leaves the stack | Fixed; red `83b3b972`, green `b09b6473` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test chosen_x_controller_shield_red -- --nocapture`. It failed to compile because `Effect::AddControllerDamageShieldEqualToChosenXUntilEndOfTurn` did not exist. The generic effect now requires the ordinary spell-level chosen-X receipt, installs an independent controller-targeted shield only for positive X, then allows later damage to consume no more than that amount. The focused contracts prove `X=2` creates the player shield, draws, prevents two of a later four damage, and leaves life at 18; `X=0` draws successfully while recording no invalid zero-valued shield. Engine all-target strict Clippy and the existing chosen-X provenance invariant pass. |
| `bathe-in-light-chosen-protection-color-not-reconciled` | RAV White/core choice gap — Bathe in Light is catalog-only, and policy moves cannot submit a spell color choice that the stack can retain and use to grant temporary controller-team protection. | Fixed; RAV red `2a3b2eff`, core red `d4b3ce6a`, core green `1ad15274`, RAV green `df5ca29c` | Exact RAV red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test bathe_in_light_red -- --nocapture` failed at `Bathe in Light definition exists`. The final public trace records `SpellColorChosen(Blue) → SpellCast → PolicyMoveSubmitted →` two friendly `ContinuousEffectCreated` receipts `→ SpellResolved`; the opponent's creature remains unprotected and a Blue targeting spell is rejected before it can mutate. |
| `twilight-drover-leaves-battlefield-token-missing` | RAV White/core coverage gap — Twilight Drover was catalog-only, and the engine had no executable definition, generic “another creature leaves the battlefield” trigger, or typed flying Spirit activation. | Fixed; red `2a99309d`, core green `c7a37b76`, RAV green `3dd40575`, scenario/catalog `f08da11a`/`5663f62b` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test twilight_drover_red -- --nocapture`; it failed at `Twilight Drover definition exists`. The final focused 2/2 trace casts Clutch at Watchwolf, records `CardMoved(Hand) → TriggeredAbilityStacked → CounterPlaced`, accepts the optional trigger, then pays `{2}{W}` for `AbilityActivated → TokenCreated` with one white Flying Spirit and `validate_invariants()`. Public scenario `rav_twilight_drover_leave_and_spirit` is digest `fnv1a64:f8f38467f5ac2e2d`; collector #33 is executable and the catalog partition is reconciled. |
| `another-creature-leaves-battlefield-trigger-missing` | Core trigger/state-machine gap — the engine only observed another creature dying, so a permanent could not generically trigger when another creature was bounced, exiled, or otherwise left the battlefield. | Fixed; red `2bda9c51`, green `c7a37b76` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test another_creature_leaves_battlefield_red -- --nocapture`; it failed to compile because `TriggerCondition::AnotherCreatureLeavesBattlefield` was absent. The generic condition is now registered and queued before any battlefield departure (including token cessation), preserving source incarnation/colors and excluding the departing creature. Synthetic core evidence is 2/2 for a non-death bounce and counter placement; RAV uses the same path. |
| `spell-color-choice-stack-provenance-missing` | Core decision/state-machine gap — a spell has no policy-facing, explicit color-choice cast action, no retained stack provenance for that choice, and no receipt proving it was selected before ordinary spell resolution. | Fixed; core red `d4b3ce6a`, core green `1ad15274` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test spell_color_choice_red -- --nocapture` failed to compile with missing `Effect::AddChosenColorProtectionToControllerCreaturesUntilEndOfTurn`, `PolicyAction::CastWithColorChoice`, and `GameEvent::SpellColorChosen`. The green path rejects omitted and Colorless choices atomically, retains one five-color choice on the spell stack object, emits its receipt before `SpellCast`, copies it into a virtual copy, forbids ability fabrication, and validates the receipt/provenance match. |
| `rav-full-fidelity-manifest-contract-stale-after-promotions` | RAV integration defect — the exact public positive-manifest assertion was not reconciled with previously merged Wojek Apothecary and Conclave's Blessing promotions, so strict RAV Clippy could not compile the full target set. | Fixed; RAV green `df5ca29c` | Exact discovery command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo clippy -p cardbench-magic-rav --all-targets -- -D warnings` failed with `can't compare [&str; 156] with [&str; 153]` in `full_fidelity_simple_spells_contract.rs`. The fixture now matches the exported manifest, including those two already-promoted IDs and Bathe in Light; strict RAV Clippy passes. |
| `zero-change-aura-attachment-invariant-rejects-legal-state` | Engine attachment/invariant defect — a legal Aura with no continuous modifier cannot resolve because receipt replay assumes every attachment created a layer effect | Fixed; red `975fe7b6`, green `6c35bcc6` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test pure_aura_attachment_red -- --nocapture`. The synthetic zero-cost Aura with `AttachSourceToTarget { changes: [] }` reached resolution, then `pass_priority` returned `IllegalAction("attachment receipt is not paired with its final continuous effect")`. The repair permits a typed binding with an empty change set and records `AttachmentEstablishedWithoutContinuousEffect` instead of overloading the layer-backed Aura/Equipment receipts. Replay continues to require a preceding matching `ContinuousEffectCreated` for every nonempty attachment, and rejects the zero-change receipt if it falsely follows one. Focused proof: the pure-Aura test is 2/2 and the existing general attachment lifecycle test is 3/3. |
| `cyclopean-snare-full-promotion-oracle-drift` | RAV card-fidelity defect — high severity, the full promotion gives Cyclopean Snare the wrong cast cost and activation, and omits its resolution-time self return | Fixed; red `47a67d7c`, green `429015fa` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test multicolor_artifact_oracle_correction_red -- --nocapture`. It failed 5/5: the definition reported `{3}` rather than printed `{2}`; the bound activation reported `{1}, {T}` rather than `{3}, {T}`; and Snare remained on the battlefield. The green repair has `{2}` and `{3}, {T}`, and uses expansion-neutral `ReturnSourceToOwnersHand`: `AbilityManaPaid({3}) → AbilityActivated → PermanentTapped(target) → CardMoved(Hand) → ObjectIncarnationAdvanced → AbilityResolved`. Its departed-source regression proves the old ability does not return a source that left the battlefield before resolution. |
| `grifters-blade-full-promotion-oracle-drift` | RAV card-fidelity / cast-timing defect — high severity, the full promotion gives Grifter's Blade the wrong mana cost, omits Flash, and omits the enter-the-battlefield attachment trigger | Fixed; red `47a67d7c`, green `429015fa` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test multicolor_artifact_oracle_correction_red -- --nocapture`. It reported printed `{3}` as `{1}` and no `Flash`; with three legal mana sources during the opponent's upkeep the cast failed `IllegalAction("non-instant spells require your main phase with an empty stack")`. The green repair provides `{3}` and typed `Flash`, then registers a target-bearing ETB attachment through the shared Equipment lifecycle. Its public trace records the Flash cast, target decision, `TriggeredAbilityStacked`, `ContinuousEffectCreated`, `EquipmentAttached`, and `AbilityResolved`; with no legal controlled creature, the Blade remains unattached. |

| `virtual-spell-copy-cannot-be-countered` | Engine stack lifecycle/invariant defect — high severity, a live virtual instant or sorcery copy is represented as `TargetRequirement::Spell` but cannot be targeted by an ordinary counter spell | Fixed; red `2eff59e1`, green `93b1fe3a` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test virtual_spell_copy_counter_red -- --nocapture`. It failed `counter may target a virtual spell copy: IllegalTarget(Spell(ObjectId(4)))`. The target matcher recognized the stack-only copy, but the post-cast invariant looked up its definition only in physical-object/departed-definition maps, so the legal cast rolled back before resolution. The green repair makes stack-target shape validation virtual-definition-aware and routes ordinary spell counters through one terminal helper. A physical spell still records `SpellCountered → CardMoved`; a virtual copy instead records `SpellCopyCountered { copy, original, source }`, is removed from the virtual stack map, and has no zone move. The focused green trace leaves only the physical original on the stack and ends with a valid invariant audit. |
| `single-target-delayed-exile-return-missing` | Engine delayed-zone-transition gap — high severity, artifact, land, and spell effects cannot exile one target creature and return that exact object at end step unless an Aura-linked group happens to be available | Fixed; red `4e59c7fd`, green `362fefce` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test exile_target_creature_delayed_return_red -- --nocapture`. It failed to compile because `Effect::ExileTargetCreatureUntilEndStep` was absent. The green substrate factors source-agnostic single-primary delayed group scheduling out of the Aura-specific path. The target moves to exile through the ordinary zone transition, captures its *post-exile* incarnation in a one-member `LinkedExileGroup`, and schedules the existing typed end-step action. At end step it returns only if still in that exact exile incarnation, under its owner’s control; source departure never cancels it. Focused evidence sees `CardMoved(Exile) → DelayedActionScheduled → CardMoved(Battlefield) → DelayedActionConsumed`, and the preexisting Aura-linked group contract remains green. |
| `leashling-hand-to-library-cost-missing` | RAV artifact/core coverage gap — Leashling was catalog-only because an activated ability could not move a policy-selected owned hand card to the top of its controller's library as an atomic cost before its source-relative owner-hand return resolved | Fixed; red `7b82f99a`, green `9d2b572a` | Exact red command: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-rav --test leashling_red -- --nocapture` failed 0/1 at `Leashling definition exists`. The repair adds a generic exact-arity `put_hand_cards_on_library_top` cost profile and public `PolicyAction::ActivateAbilityWithGeneralizedCosts`; duplicate, foreign, non-hand, and discard-overlapping choices roll back atomically. The focused trace is `HandCardPutOnLibraryTopAsAbilityCost → CardMoved(Library) → ObjectIncarnationAdvanced → AbilityActivated → PriorityPassed ×2 → CardMoved(Leashling, Hand) → AbilityResolved`. A Last Gasp response moves the old source to its owner's graveyard and the resolving historical ability does not return that later incarnation; final invariant audit passes. |
| `triggered-sacrifice-derived-control-mismatch` | Engine triggered-choice/control lifecycle defect — high severity, every “sacrifice a creature you control” trigger while its controller controls an opponent-owned permanent | Fixed; red `d09b2893`, green `9e9b8b54` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test triggered_sacrifice_derived_control_red -- --nocapture`. An upkeep trigger opened a sacrifice choice after a layer-two effect gave its controller control of an opposing creature. Candidate construction used derived `controller_of`, but the pending-decision invariant recomputed candidates from the immutable base `CardObject::controller`, causing the resolution transition to roll back with `IllegalAction("trigger-effect decision escaped its typed continuation boundary")`. The repair uses the same live derived-control predicate for choice revalidation and final sacrifice legality. Green evidence is `ContinuousEffectCreated → ControllerChanged → DecisionOpened → DecisionCompleted → SacrificedByEffect → CardMoved(Graveyard) → ContinuousEffectExpired → AbilityResolved`; the card goes to its owner’s graveyard and the final invariant audit passes. |
| `activated-ability-control-change-stack-provenance` | Engine stack/control-change invariant defect — high severity, any activated ability answered by a control-changing effect | Fixed; red `2ef9655f`, green `46973b16` | Exact red command from `varieties/magic`: `cargo test -p cardbench-magic-engine --test activated_ability_control_change_red -- --nocapture`. Before the repair, a player activated a one-damage ability and the opponent answered with `GainControlTargetUntilEndOfTurn`; the second pass to resolve that response failed `IllegalAction("a battlefield ability source must remain controlled by its activator")`. The repair removes the invalid live-controller equality from the stack invariant: CR 113.7a makes the activation controller historical stack provenance. Green event evidence is `AbilityActivated(player 0) → SpellCast(player 1) → ContinuousEffectCreated(Control) → ControllerChanged(0→1) → SpellResolved → DamageDealtToPlayer(player 1, 1) → AbilityResolved`; the original activator still controls the older ability despite the source being stolen. |
